#2838. 3 Iowa wrestling things we learned at Hawkeyes' media day

IOWA CITY − The Iowa wrestling season is fast approaching, with the season-opening dual against Bellarmine coming on Nov. 13.

The program's annual day was held on Nov. 4, and reporters heard from coach Tom Brands and his wrestlers before the season begins. The Hawkeyes aim to build off of a fourth-place finish at last season's NCAA Championships and will be replacing several key figures — Drake Ayala, Michael Caliendo and Patrick Kennedy.

Brands praised wrestlers like Gabe Arnold and Ben Kueter for their accomplishments last season, while also emphasizing they need to continue to make leaps if they are to catch Penn State and other top programs in the program's chase for a national title.

So far, Brands said Iowa is attacking the challenge well.

"Our veterans have been conscientious about getting better," Brands said. "That's a good characteristic. Why is that important and why is that a good characteristic? Because a lot of times when you have accomplished something more than once... like Kueter is a two-time All-American, he's got two years left, he might think he's an automatic. He's the No. 1 heavyweight in America right now, ranked, at least in one or two polls that I've seen. That doesn't mean he's the No. 1 heavyweight in America. He still has to prove that every time out."

Here are the three biggest things we learned from the Hawkeyes' media day.

The Hawkeyes had several injuries last year. Add in Lehigh transfer and NCAA All-American Ryan Crookham to the mix, who had his previous two seasons cut short by injury, and there was a lot to be learned. Ultimately, Crookham, DeLuca, Ferrari and Kueter all expressed confidence in how they were feeling going into the season-opener.

DeLuca said he felt "strong, fully healthy," and is feeling added gratefulness as he returns to the mat. He said his rehab went well and allowed him to return in an efficient manner following his season-ending injury against Missouri last November.

For Crookham, Brands praised how he is looking in practice. Crookham said he felt good and is taking his recovery day by day with the coaches. He also confirmed he will have two years of eligibility remaining.

Ferrari said that he felt "really healthy," and "fantastic," as the season approaches, while also having an added fire for falling short in the NCAA semifinals last season.

Finally, Kueter said he is fully healthy coming into the season. He had meniscus surgery right after the NCAA Championships, but got on the mat and lifting two to three weeks later. He said it's the first time since he's been in college that he could train an entire summer to get ready for the season.

It appears that Iowa has a relatively clean bill of health entering the year, which will be key with limited depth of a 30-man roster cap and the need for some of the program's heaviest hitters to be their best come March.

Ayala, Caliendo and Kennedy were all cornerstones of the program last year, both in how they wrestled and how they led the team. It brings up a key question of who may be taking that mantle now.

Brands credited his veterans like Kueter, Ferrari, Arnold, Ryder Block, Nasir Bailey and Crookham for upholding the standard Iowa expects of itself and making sure all 30 wrestlers are marching toward the same goals.

"To keep answering your question about how have these guys changed or what characteristic does this team have as a whole, they're hearing more from their teammates on, 'Hey, that's not going to fly,'" Brands said.

That idea of everyone marching to the same drum was a topic new assistant coach Jacob Warner talked about in an interview with the Des Moines Register in September. One of the traits his national title-winning team had in 2021 was a roster in lockstep about what they wanted. He felt that was one of their main reasons why they could win a team title.

It's something the entire team is embracing, Arnold said. He mentioned an instance where DeLuca challenged him for slacking off during a hill-running workout. While Arnold jokingly denied taking a rep off, he loved the challenge from someone like DeLuca, who is only in his second year of the program.

"Everyone's stepped up a little bit, it's really cool to see," Arnold said.

It'll be interesting to see if that attitude translates into more wins for the Hawkeyes, but it is a trait embraced by Hawkeye teams in the past that led to success.

One of the larger questions of the Hawkeye lineup was left in the air to an extent at 149 or 157 pounds, but some questions were answered. Jordan Williams, who was charged with operating while under the influence in September will serve a one-match suspension, missing the dual vs. Bellarmine on Nov. 13, according to Brands.

Aside from the off-the-mat incident, Williams had an intriguing summer as well. He initially entered the transfer portal, but later returned to the Hawkeyes. He also had an inspired run of freestyle action, making the U23 World team for the United States and finding success at Senior-level events.

"He chose to stay and the standard is in place," Brands said. "We all have to do better and he knows he has to do better. We're going to help him do better, but in this sport, which is individual by nature, you have to do better."

Ambiguity remains on how lineup battles will shake out. Williams, Block and Kael Voinovich are all listed at 149 pounds. Miguel Estrada is at 157 pounds. Block is a returning NCAA All-American at 149 pounds, but it appears Williams and Voinovich may challenge for the spot. Brands said they are approaching the end of their wrestle-offs and should have more clarity on the lineup soon, but said all four contribute to solid depth at the weights.

'We have our best depth at 149 and 157," Brands said. "There's four guys there with Voinovich and Estrada, then you have Block who's a returning all-American and you have Jordan Williams. Those four guys at 149 and 157, they're going to factor in and we're going to need them all."

Eli McKown covers high school sports and wrestling for the Des Moines Register. Contact him atEmckown@gannett.com. Follow him on Twitter at @EMcKown23.

#2837. Warriors reportedly not paying Kristaps Porziņģis as he contemplates NBA future

Kristaps Porziņģis is reportedly not being paid any of his $20 million salary by the Golden State Warriors since he is not playing, according to ESPN's Shams Charania and Anthony Slater.

Porziņģis, who is away from the team as he deals with recurring POTS (Postural Orthostatic Tachycardia Syndrome) symptoms, remains in play as the Warriors seek to move the 31-year-old forward before the Feb. 11 NBA trade deadline while they continue not paying him.

There are reportedly no plans for the Warriors to void Porziņģis' contract and the player is not disputing the lack of payments. He signed a two-year, $40 million contract in the summer with only $3 million guaranteed in the second season of the deal.

According to Bobby Marks, the decision is costing Porziņģis $218,341 per game.

Porziņģis released a statement last month saying that he was working with doctors to find a way back to better health after Charania reported the veteran was contemplating his future.

According to Cleveland Clinic, "POTS is a condition that causes your heart to beat faster than normal when you go from sitting or lying down to standing up." Symptoms can vary widely from patient to patient, but Porziņģis' seem more respiratory and fatigue-related.

POTS significantly limited the 7-foot-2 Porziņģis during the 2024-25 Eastern Conference semifinals against the New York Knicks, when he was with the Boston Celtics.

Porziņģis averaged just 15.5 minutes in that six-game series, which ended Boston's NBA title defense, with Celtics head coach Joe Mazzulla revealing after Game 5 that Porziņģis didn't play the second half because "he couldn't breathe."

Over his 10-year career, Porziņģis has played 533 games and has averaged 19.5 points, 7.6 rebounds and 1.9 assists per game.

Last season, he split time between the Atlanta Hawks and the Warriors, appearing in 32 games, the fewest he's played in a season in his career outside of the missed 2018-19 campaign.

#2834. Jets’ Breece Hall Gets Major Trade Prediction Ahead of NFL Deadline

The New York Jets face a decision about their backfield as the NFL trade deadline approaches, with Breece Hall emerging as a potential trade candidate. A growing role for younger running back Braelon Allen has raised questions about whether the team should move its established starter and prioritize its longer-term rebuilding plans.

Allen has delivered an impressive stretch during Hall's absence, giving the struggling offense a productive option and creating a new challenge for the coaching staff. In a standout performance, Allen rushed for 147 yards on 21 carries and scored on a 21-yard run. His production has made it increasingly difficult to overlook the possibility that he can handle a larger role.

Nov 30, 2025; East Rutherford, New Jersey, USA; New York Jets running back Breece Hall (20) practices before the game at MetLife Stadium. Mandatory Credit: Vincent Carchietta-Imagn Images

The opportunity has also highlighted the difference in cost and workload between the two backs. Allen is playing on an inexpensive contract and has accumulated relatively few NFL touches, while the veteran has handled more than 1,000 career touches and dealt with injuries, including a torn ACL in 2022. Allen has recorded 504 rushing yards across his past five starts, a span that underscores his value to the offense.

The Jets, however, have maintained that their established starter remains part of their plans. Coach Aaron Glenn has indicated that the role will not change when the veteran returns, while offensive coordinator Frank Reich has described the presence of two capable backs as a positive situation. That stance suggests the organization is not actively preparing to move him, despite the outside speculation.

Oct 26, 2025; Cincinnati, Ohio, USA; New York Jets running back Breece Hall (20) celebrates the win against the Cincinnati Bengals at Paycor Stadium. Mandatory Credit: Joseph Maiorana-Imagn Images

Hall is projected to be traded to a new team before the NFL trade deadline this year. This is detailed more in an analysis by Tyler Sullivan for CBS Sports.

Hall fits the bill as a player who is more valuable to them as a trade asset rather than a contributor on the field. That's thanks, in part, to the emergence of fellow running back Braelon Allen.

As Hall has been sidelined with a quad injury, Allen has flourished in the starting role. Over the last five games with Hall on the shelf, Allen has started in each contest and has rushed for 504 yards on a 5.4 yards-per-carry average and has two rushing touchdowns. Allen is also averaging 3.92 yards per carry after contact over this stretch, which is elite. For reference, that's better than Derrick Henry (3.11) and Jahmyr Gibbs (3.39) this season.

A trade could nevertheless make sense if New York receives an attractive offer. The team reportedly turned down a third-round selection from Kansas City last year, and another deal would likely need to provide comparable value. Hall's contract is not necessarily an obstacle, as its structure could allow the Jets to create cap flexibility by moving him.

Allen's recent performances give New York a credible alternative in the backfield, while additional draft capital could help the franchise address broader roster needs. The Jets must decide whether keeping an established playmaker in Hall is more valuable than acquiring future assets. Any move should reflect the return they receive rather than simply making a change before the deadline.

#2832. 49ers will have to make a decision on Brandon Aiyuk, sooner or later

With 49ers receiver Brandon Aiyuk finally doing what he should have done months ago, the ball will return to the team’s court.

The process for being reinstated from the reserve/left squad list is believed to be administrative. (Then again, the process of applying for the supplemental draft was believed to be administrative, too; Brendan Sorsby learned otherwise.) Once the league reinstates Aiyuk, the 49ers will have to put him on the roster, trade him, or cut him.

The 49ers could emerge from the situation with a roster exemption, which would buy them some time — and which would force Aiyuk to follow through on the obligation to show up, take a physical, and see what happens next.

The 49ers would then have options. Aiyuk would have to take a physical. If he fails, the 49ers could deem it to be a non-football injury. That would allow them to park him on the reserve/NFI list and not pay him.

They could try to trade him before Tuesday’s deadline, but no trade would happen without Aiyuk restructuring his contract to the league minimum, or something close to it. A trade seems highly unlikely, given that his 2026 base salary is $26.150 million.

The simplest outcome would be for the 49ers to cut him, and to let him sign wherever he wants. (If they cut him after the trade deadline, he’d have to pass through waivers. No one would claim his contract as it’s currently written.)

The biggest question is whether the 49ers will simply wash their hands of Aiyuk or play hardball with him. Hard feelings linger, given the way the relationship disintegrated.

It all comes down to whether the 49ers want to spend the time and effort necessary to make it as difficult as they can for him to get what he clearly wants: The ability to sign with any team he chooses.

He has said he wants to play for the Commanders. It’s still unknown whether the Commanders would want him. It’s also unknown whether anyone else would want him.

None of that matters until the 49ers release him. It’s possible, in theory, that they try to find a way to keep him on ice without paying him.

#2830. Binance breach puts custody at center of crypto infrastructure trade

The $6.3 billion Binance hack could accelerate a shift in how investors value cryptocurrency companies, putting a greater premium on custody providers and the infrastructure needed to securely hold and protect digital assets.

Much of crypto's evolution this year has involved companies moving past their reliance on trading volumes and transaction fees, to focus instead on building the infrastructure connecting digital assets with traditional finance. Last Thursday's breach adds a new dimension, bringing cybersecurity, custody, compliance and balance-sheet resilience into sharper focus.

Importantly, the Binance breach was not an indictment of crypto itself, or a case of leadership-driven fraud, as with FTX in late 2022. Instead, Binance's leadership moved quickly to contain the fallout, protect customers and commit the exchange's own resources to cover losses.

On Tuesday, Binance CEO Richard Teng told CNBC's "Squawk on the Street" that the company has replenished 98% of $6.34 billion in affected assets, and that it is still in the process of purchasing outstanding, more illiquid assets.

Management's response, alongside efforts elsewhere in the industry to boost confidence, helped prevent a security breach from escalating into a broader crisis of confidence. For example, Tether, Circle, Ripple and others moved quickly to freeze or restrict access to about $3 billion in stolen assets; Coinbase blocked transfers to compromised wallets and offered Binance direct assistance.

The hack could even help shape the next phase of the crypto infrastructure trade. The new winners may not just be the businesses with the most users, volume or assets under custody, but the ones that can also demonstrate they're able to protect those assets under extreme duress.

"Which company has the resources to invest in cybersecurity and protect customer funds? For a smaller company, it could be much harder because once these crypto companies become infrastructure plays, providing the well for tokenization … they need to have the compliance and control in place so that a similar hacking will not happen or can be minimized," Owen Lau, senior analyst at ClearStreet, told CNBC. "Larger companies have more resources that could give them some advantage."

While institutional investors have increasingly embraced digital assets, broader adoption depends on confidence that those assets can be securely held, accessed and transferred. The Binance breach highlights the importance of that infrastructure, and could increase demand for specialized custodians and platforms with established asset-protection capabilities.

Coinbase has operated a dedicated institutional custody business since 2018. Circle entered the custody business this year, after the U.S. Office of the Comptroller of the Currency granted it approval to operate as a trust bank. In the past few years, Ripple and Kraken have also launched custody businesses.

Still, smaller players compete in the space as well. Small cap crypto exchange stock Gemini Space Station has offered custody services since 2019. BitGo Holdings, founded in 2013, is one of the earliest players in institutional crypto custody and infrastructure, with a reputation for security and serving major institutions.

The shift could also drive consolidation as companies look to buy capabilities, deepen their infrastructure offerings and achieve the scale needed to compete, Lau said.

The more consequential structural shift may be the separation of custody from execution, according to Morgan Stanley.

"Crypto exchanges have historically combined trading, custody, and settlement within vertically integrated platforms, concentrating operational risk. Repeated security breaches could encourage investors to move assets toward independent custody while continuing to trade on CEXs," the firm's analyst Felix Stratmann said in a note Monday," referring to centralized cryptocurrency exchanges.

"This would preserve the role of centralized exchanges as liquidity and execution venues, while potentially benefiting regulated custodians, including banks, as institutional investors place greater emphasis on asset segregation, operational resilience, and investor protection," Stratmann said.

#2826. Report: Warriors withholding payments to Kristaps Porzingis on $20M salary

The strange saga concerning the playing status of veteran forward Kristaps Porzingis continues, as the Golden State Warriors are reportedly not paying his $20 million salary for the 2025-26 season because he is not playing.

ESPN.com reported on Wednesday, Nov. 4 that the non-payment stems from Porzingis not rendering services to the team or fulfilling his contract. Porzingis has not played yet this season because of an undisclosed illness that the team has yet to clarify.

The Warriors can seek to trade Porzingis before the Feb. 11 trade deadline, as Porzingis has reportedly leaned toward retirement. The salary for the 31-year-old still counts against the salary cap and any tax apron that the Warriors could possibly pay.

Porzingis' two-year, $40 million deal expires at season's end, making him a prime trade candidate and allowing any acquiring team to shed salary on its own roster to create a roster spot, possibly for a playoff push. According to the report, Golden State is not currently entertaining voiding the remainder of his contract.

The Hawks traded Porzingis to Golden State for Jonathan Kuminga and Buddy Hield on Feb. 5, 2026. He played only 15 games with the Warriors and 32 games overall in the 2025-26 season.

Golden State saw enough potential to sign him to a two-year, $40 million extension in June, which came with a player option for the 2027-28 season. He was guaranteed $20 million for this season, but only $3 million was guaranteed in the last year of the deal.

Before training camp started, general manager Mike Dunleavy was called by Porzingis, who informed him he was stepping away from the team.

The team then said Porzingis would be around as he recovered from his illness, but he wouldn't play because of that medical condition.

In 12 NBA seasons, Porzingis has averaged 19.5 points and 7.6 rebounds for the New York Knicks, Dallas Mavericks, Washington Wizards and Boston Celtics, along with the Hawks and Warriors.

#2823. Como becomes 1st away team to score 9 goals in a Champions League match

LENS, France (AP) — Como became the first away team to score nine goals in a Champions League match with a 9-1 rout at Lens on Wednesday.

Cesc Fabregas' team made it three victories in four matches of its debut season in the competition and moved up to fourth in the league phase standings.

"Today everything went our way," Fabregas said. "A great night for Como and our fans."

The previous best scoring performance by an away club was Bayern Munich's 8-2 win over Barcelona at a neutral venue (Lisbon) in 2020.

It matched the biggest margin of victory after Liverpool and Real Madrid's 8-0 home victories against Besiktas and Malmo, respectively.

Tasos Douvikas had a hat trick and has now scored in all four of Como's matches. He leads the Champions League with six goals.

Nico Paz also found the net and then instead of celebrating went and grabbed a T-shirt featuring an image of Jesús de Miguel, the Tenerife forward who died following an on-field collision with the opposing team's goalkeeper.

Paz spent time in Tenerife's youth system from 2014-16.

Lucas Da Cunha, Marc-Oliver Kempf, Mattia Liberali, Martina Baturina and Matteo Papaccioli also scored for Como.

The 18-year-old Papaccioli, who came on in the second half, is a member of Como's youth team and hasn't even played in Serie A yet.

#2822. Which NFL coaches are on the hot seat?

The NFL regular season is approaching its halfway point, and for some head coaches, the pressure to turn things around is becoming impossible to ignore. With playoff hopes fading for several teams, questions about leadership, accountability and the direction of struggling franchises are beginning to take center stage.

The NFL's coaching carousel is always one of the league's biggest storylines, but the decisions made over the next several weeks could determine which coaches survive and which organizations decide it's time for a change. Some coaches may still have enough time to salvage their seasons, while others are running out of opportunities to prove they're the right person for the job.

In this edition of "Safe or Fire," we'll evaluate coaches across the league whose seats are heating up, examining whether they deserve another opportunity or if their respective teams should begin looking elsewhere.

The Chargers are 0-7 and Harbaugh is 0-10 in his last 10 games. It's been tough to watch this year especially as Los Angeles had playoff expectations (and favorable preseason Super Bowl odds). Herbert looks lost for a coach known for being a quarterback whisperer.

The Jets are 5-20 under Aaron Glenn. It's almost a guarantee that a new head coach will be hired in 2027 with the team having three first round picks and likely to spend one on a new quarterback. Aaron Glenn also has seen him teams outscored significantly with under 2 minutes left in the first half. Stuff like that shows you are outcoached weekly.

There is no reason that the Houston Texans shouldn't be succeeding this year. The Texans did not really lose anything on defense yet the Texans are 2-5 despite averaging 23.5 points per game (15th in NFL). It's not the offense this time, it's the defense.

The Colts getting blown out in Week 8 to the Jaguars will probably be the final nail in the coffin for Steichen's tenure in Indianapolis. Chris Ballard was already fired mid-season. Steichen will probably finish the year but then he is gone.

The Titans are 1-7 in their first year with Saleh. They decided to trade away Jeffery Simmons because management likely sees this as a rebuild year and way to give Cam Ward a supporting cast.

I think Saleh will get another year in 2027 since the team is focused on adding picks rather than winning now.

Washington had a fortunate run to the NFC Championship in 2024. But since then, Quinn's Commanders are 7-16-1. Jayden Daniels needs an offensive minded coach to get his career on track. The disastrous ending against the New York Giants in Week 5 sums up Quinn's last 24+ games.

Here's the story on Sirianni. When the offensive coordinator is the scapegoat in 2025, there is no one else to blame now for the offensive woes. It falls squarely on Nick Sirianni and Jalen Hurts.

Sirianni relied on a young and cheap roster in his first few seasons. But now players are getting paid and the team can't hide Hurts's limitations.

Bowles is 38-38 in four plus seasons with the Buccaneers. With the NFC South being so weak, going .500 is pretty disappointing.

Dallas has Texas sized expectations. With Brian Schottenheimer failing to address its defensive woes in two straight seasons, you wonder how patient Jerry Jones will be? The bad collapses in Week 3 and Week 8 are concerning along with a dud to a Bucs team without Baker Mayfield in Week 5.

#2821. Pistons vs 76ers preview: East’s top two teams square off on National TV

If you are to believe preseason predictions, this game being a showdown between the East's top two teams could come as a bit of a surprise. Pistons fans knew this team would be good, but many talking heads predicted a falloff this season while everybody was on board with the 76ers being one of the best teams in the league.

It shouldn't come as a surprise that the team that was 1st in the East last season is once again competing for the top seed in the East, but talking heads have to make their money somehow. ESPN at least had the foresight to pickup this game and you get what should be a very fun early-season showdown between two teams that have only lost one game each.

The 76ers and Pistons have played once this season and the Pistons won, but it is tough to draw any conclusions since both teams were missing a couple of starters.

Where: Little Caesars Arena in Detroit, MIWhen: Wednesday, November 4 at 7 pm ESTWatch: ESPNOdds: Detroit (-2.5)

The Pistons will be without Jalen Duren and Isaiah Joe, who was already ruled out for this game last night. The Pistons were missing Jalen Duren in the last matchup between these teams and also Ausar Thompson, who they will have back. But, Isaiah Joe did suffer the hip injury that is still keeping him out during the 1st quarter of the last matchup, so the Pistons are trading one injured player for another.

With this being a National TV game and the 76ers getting a day of rest, I would suspect that everybody plays for them tonight. In the last matchup, the 76ers did not have Joel Embiid or VJ Edgecombe, which really put their lack of depth to the test.

Joel Embiid's size and ability on the glass could become a problem for the Pistons, who will continue to start the undersized Paul Reed and likely use John Collins to back him up. Without Jalen Duren, rebounding was already going to be a problem, but the Pistons have been able to overcome it during his absence.

In the last matchup, the Pistons were able to contain Tyrese Maxey through 3 quarters before he started hitting shots in the 4th. It was a bit of shocking development since the Pistons were without Ausar Thompson, but without some of the other stars for the 76ers, they could focus their defense more on stopping Maxey. The 76ers have a lot of star power, but their biggest offensive threat is arguably Maxey, who can hit shots from anywhere on the court and can use his quickness with the ball to get to where he wants. I would expect Ausar Thompson to draw the Tyrese Maxey matchup which will allow Cade Cunningham to guard Jaylen Brown since he has the size to keep up with him.

I am not really sure what the Pistons are going to do about the Joel Embiid matchup. Paul Reed is outsized by him and also can have some issues with fouling and Joel Embiid has made a living off of bating fouls throughout his career. Paul Reed is going to have to stay disciplined, as I don't think the Pistons can afford to have heavy minutes of John Collins at center or Tolu Smith. I have full confidence in the Pistons' ability to matchup with the rest of the 76ers starting lineup, but the most concern I have is this matchup and it very well could swing the game.

If there is one certainty for the Pistons, it is that Cade Cunningham is difficult to slow down, especially with how he is playing now. He is coming off a 33 points masterclass against the Charlotte Hornets on Monday in only 27 minutes where he absolutely took over the game and turned a single-digit lead into a 20-plus point blowout. Cade Cunningham always starts off the season slow, but he seems to be hitting his stride now. His 3-ball is consistently falling as he is shooting 37% from beyond the arc, which is a significant development for his offensive creation abilities and efficiency.

He is going to have to put together another masterclass if the Pistons want any hope to win this game. During Jalen Duren's absence, the Pistons have been able to win games through their high-level defense, but their offense has left a lot to be desired. With Isaiah Joe out and Duncan Robinson going through a bit of a cold stretch, the Pistons shooting fell off a bit and they went four games in a row scoring 110 points or less.

However, everything seemed to get back on track on Monday against the Hornets, so hopefully that continues.

One player the Pistons are hoping gets back on track is John Collins. He had his best game as a Piston in the last game against the 76ers and has proceeded to score single-digit points in every game since then. With Jalen Duren out, he has had to play a lot more center than he was probably planning on playing, but he has also had difficulties finding a consistent offensive role. Another big John Collins game would be a huge help in this matchup

The Joel Embiid matchup concerns me with the Pistons lack of frontcourt size, but if they can defend Tyrese Maxey like they did in the last matchup, they have a shot to takedown the 76ers and claim the top seed in the Eastern Conference.

Detroit Pistons (6-1): Cade Cunningham, Duncan Robinson, Ausar Thompson, John Collins, Paul Reed

Philadelphia 76ers (7-1): Tyrese Maxey, VJ Edgecombe, Jaylen Brown, LeBron James, Joel Embiid

Which of these teams will finish higher in the Eastern Conference at the end of the season?

#2820. Dan Campbell and Jeff Hafley agree: Dolphins now are like Lions at start of their rebuild

In Dan Campbell’s first season as the Lions’ head coach, he went 3-13-1. By Year 2 the Lions had improved to 9-8, and in Year 3 they were in the NFC Championship Game. Dolphins coach Jeff Hafley wants to build his team the way Campbell built the Lions, and Campbell thinks Hafley is doing it the right way.

With the Dolphins set to face the Lions on Sunday, both Hafley and Campbell said today that they see similarities between where the Dolphins are now and where the Lions were in Campbell’s first season: Building a winning culture, even if their record doesn’t show it yet.

“That’s how I want to play football. Let me retract that: That’s how we are playing football,” Hafley said of the way the Lions play. “I think if you watch us right now, every coach has said it, and everyone who watches us, whether it’s on TV or they turn on the tape, I get texts or phone calls, that’s what’s showing up on tape right now. And while it’s disappointing that we’re 1-6, the foundation of that with young guys, that’s really important to us. When you see the physicality and the toughness and how hard guys are playing, it means that they’re playing for each other and they’re bought in. We’ve brought the right guys here. That’s very, very important to me, and it will continue to be.”

Campbell said he thinks Hafley is building his team exactly the way a new coach should.

“Coach Hafley’s done a good job in his first year,” Campbell said. “A lot of similarities — it reminds me of what we were when we came in. They’re young, they’re hungry, they’re active, they run, they hit, they hustle, they finish, try to play disciplined ball. And they’re playing that way.”

#2816. Jalen Hurts returns to Eagles facility, provides morale boost to team

The Philadelphia Eagles received encouraging news Wednesday, as quarterback Jalen Hurts returned to the team's practice facility for the first time in a while while continuing to deal with a serious medical condition that has sidelined him indefinitely.

According to ESPN's Tim McManus, Hurts was at the Eagles' facility Wednesday and attended the team meeting along with other meetings. Although the veteran quarterback did not address his teammates, his presence boosted morale for some players who had not seen him since Philadelphia placed him on injured reserve Oct. 17. The development comes during a difficult stretch for the Eagles, who have lost six consecutive games and fallen to 2-6 heading into Sunday's NFC East matchup against the New York Giants. Philadelphia has struggled to find consistency on offense while dealing with a series of injuries, including the loss of its franchise quarterback.

Hurts has been sidelined since being diagnosed with a blood clot in his calf, with subsequent medical evaluations raising additional concerns. His condition has made his health and recovery the organization's top priority, and he is expected to miss the rest of the season.

While Wednesday's appearance does not signal a change in Hurts' medical status or suggest an imminent return to football activities, it represents an encouraging development for a locker room that has endured significant adversity.

Hurts has been one of Philadelphia's most important leaders throughout his tenure, providing stability at quarterback while helping establish the team's expectations and competitive identity.

His absence has been felt beyond the football field, particularly as the Eagles have struggled to regain their footing following a promising start to the season.

Head coach Nick Sirianni previously acknowledged how difficult it has been to be without his starting quarterback, emphasizing their relationship and expressing concern for Hurts' well-being.

The Eagles have also been forced to adjust their quarterback situation since Hurts landed on injured reserve. Tanner McKee started the past three games, but Philadelphia is making another change, turning to Joe Milton for Sunday's matchup against the Giants.

With the Eagles approaching their Week 10 bye, the organization is trying to stabilize a season that has quickly moved in the wrong direction. Philadelphia remains searching for answers on offense, and the upcoming matchup provides another opportunity to end its losing streak.

For Hurts, the focus remains on his recovery rather than any potential return to the lineup. However, his appearance at the facility Wednesday gave teammates an opportunity to reconnect with their quarterback and provided a positive moment during an otherwise frustrating season.

The Eagles still have significant problems to solve on the field, but seeing their franchise quarterback back around the team offered something that has been in short supply recently: an encouraging development.

#2806. Colts are not planning to rest Jonathan Taylor or limit his workload as trade rumors swirl

INDIANAPOLIS (AP) — Indianapolis Colts coach Shane Steichen insisted Wednesday that Pro Bowl running back Jonathan Taylor's workload would not change this weekend against the Dallas Cowboys.

Instead, Steichen, Taylor and the Colts say they're still playing to win.

Taylor carried eight times in last week's embarrassing 42-7 loss at Jacksonville, a noticeable decrease from his previous season low of 15 carries at Pittsburgh and tied for the fifth-fewest in his seven-year career.

Still, Steichen said the team was not protecting its top trade asset from injury and would not on the final Sunday before next week's trade deadline.

"I think there's a lot of stuff out there right now, but he's on our football and he's excited for this opportunity to play. He wants to go win this game," Steichen said. "There's a lot of hypotheticals out there on the trade talks right now, but he's on our football team."

Taylor does not typically take questions on Wednesday. He's expected to speak with reporters as usual Thursday.

The 27-year-old old Taylor has been a hot topic on the trading block with speculation rapidly increasing after general manager Chris Ballard was fired two weeks and longtime teammates Quenton Nelson and DeForest Buckner were dealt for draft picks last week.

During last week's interview, the 27-year-old Taylor, who finished third in the league in rushing in 2025 and is fifth this year, made an impassioned plea for players to show fans the Colts (2-6) have not given up on this season — even though the personnel moves clearly signaled team co-owner Carlie Irsay-Gordon was ready to rebuild next season.

After Sunday's debacle, Taylor again urged his teammates to stay focused on winning now rather than contemplating the franchise's future plans or whether he would be on the roster beyond this week's game.

"Teams are going to look at us even more, foaming at the mouth," the team captain said in Jacksonville. "Your back is against the wall, what are you going to do? You're going to have to fight your way out of a corner. This is not getting nowhere near any easier, you've got to accept that, and when you accept that, that's just the first step."

Indy already made one move for 2027 by re-signing defensive tackle Adetomiwa Adebawore. His two-year contract is worth $35 million with $22 million guaranteed and a $13 million signing bonus and it keeps him off the free-agent market.

It's been a breakout season for Adebawore, who already has matched his career high with four sacks.

"I wanted to be here, so Carlie came up to me and we went through the negotiations and I felt very comfortable with the situation," Adebawore said. "I'm glad we got it done, I'm excited for this week."

How the Colts intend to proceed remains unclear, other than the fact Irsay-Gordon is content to collect draft picks and shed salary cap so Indy can be more active in the offseason and perhaps more attractive to Ballard's replacement since they don't have a first-round pick in 2027. Ballard sent that pick to the New York Jets last November for All-Pro cornerback Sauce Gardner.

Wednesday marked the anniversary of that deal, one that has not worked out the way Indy had hoped. The Colts were a league-best 8-2 when they added Gardner to help end a then four-year playoff drought and make a deep postseason run.

But Gardner got hurt, quarterback Daniel Jones suffered a broken bone in his left leg and a torn right Achilles tendon, and the Colts didn't win another game.

They're 2-13 since that trade, including a home loss when they were serenaded with boos and chants of "Fire Ballard!" in their last home game, something Gardner hopes to change.

"It's not going exactly how I wanted it to go, obviously, because it goes without saying I wanted to go the playoffs last year," Gardner said. "Same thing this year. We've got a lot more football to play, but just wish we could more (wins). But we've got a unique opportunity to go out and see what we can do."

Gardner's perspective heading into the Tuesday deadline also is interesting because he was in this same situation last year and sat out his final Jets game with a concussion.

Now the question is whether anyone will sit for Indy?

Steichen has not wavered in his support of Daniel Jones as Indy's starting quarterback despite this season's penchant for turnovers and the possibility he could be attractive for a quarterback-needy team with one year left on his $88 million contract.

Receiver Josh Downs also is available after requesting a trade last week. He's expected to face his brother, Cowboys safety Caleb Downs, on Sunday.

Steichen, whose job also is in jeopardy, noted he's worried about his status with the organization and hasn't been told by anyone in the front office who he should play — or who he shouldn't.

"I think all the guys that are on our football team are getting ready to go play a football game," he said. "Those are the conversations I've had with those guys (in the front office), that we're going out to try to go win a football game right now. That's where we're at."

#2805. Is the Lakers’ red-hot offense sustainable?

Back-to-back wins helped get the taste of a really frustrating loss out of the mouths of Lakers fans to start the week. The absurd play of Luka Dončić also helped us forget as well.

It's a fun time to be a Lakers fan, evidenced by Bill Simmons' anger about the Lakers. Anything to take his focus off Jayson Tatum and Jaylen Brown feuding, I guess.

We asked again for questions from you guys and, once again, you answered…or asked? Either way, there's plenty to talk about in this week's mailbag!

It might feel like the Lakers have an unsustainable offense, but I think some pretty encouraging numbers push back against that. So, let's dive into this because there is a lot to this.

First, let's look at the threes. Sure, many of Luka threes don't seem sustainable except that it's Luka taking those threes. But outside of that, the team is getting quality looks from beyond the arc.

The team is ninth in 3-point rate with 46% of their shots coming from beyond the arc. That's a drastic change from last year when they ranked 22nd at 39.4%.

More notably, the team was dead last in catch-and-shoot 3-pointers attempted and made last year and 24th in 3-pointers attempted overall. This season, it hasn't even been that drastic of an increase in volume, but it has improved. They're 16th in 3-pointers attempted, 25th in catch-and-shoot 3-pointers attempted and 19th in makes.

The biggest changes are that they rank second in 3-point percentage, first in catch-and-shoot 3-point percentage and eighth in wide-open 3-point percentage. And yet, they still rank just 15th in the percentage of their points coming from threes.

That's because they're still getting to the rim, too. Last season, they were basically, across the board, the best team at scoring once they got to the paint. It's the benefit of having LeBron James with Luka and Austin Reaves, two great finishers at the rim.

LeBron is, obviously, gone now. But for this specific stat, they replaced him with Walker Kessler, who has feasted at the rim as a roll man. Add in Collin Sexton and Quentin Grimes, two players north of the 85th percentile in field goal percentage at the rim, and you see why the Lakers rank second in the league in field goal percentage at the rim and first in field goal percentage in the paint.

To perhaps lay this out a little differently, the Lakers opened the season with Walker Kessler flourishing in the pick and roll. Teams rightly decided that wasn't sustainable, so they started blitzing ballhandlers on ball screens and working to take away Kessler as a roll option.

The result is open 3-point shooters and the Lakers now have a team capable of punishing teams who leave them open. Certainly, they won't keep shooting this well, but this is also the type of offense head coach JJ Redick wants to run. He wants a modern offense that puts up lots of threes.

So, don't expect a change, but also don't be quite so worried about it being unsustainable.

LOS ANGELES, CA – NOVEMBER 1: Walker Kessler #14 of the Los Angeles Lakers blocks the shot of Collin Murray-Boyles #30 of the Toronto Raptors during the game between the Toronto Raptors and the Los Angeles Lakers on November 1, 2026 at Crypto.Com Arena in Los Angeles, California. NOTE TO USER: User expressly acknowledges and agrees that, by downloading and/or using this Photograph, user is consenting to the terms and conditions of the Getty Images License Agreement. Mandatory Copyright Notice: Copyright 2026 NBAE (Photo by Juan Ocampo/NBAE via Getty Images) | NBAE via Getty Images

Let's jump to the other side of the floor now and look at the defense.

The good news is that when you have an offense as good as the Lakers have had, you don't need to be great defensively to win. The bad news is, notably, bad defenses don't typically win at the highest levels.

As commenters pointed out, two areas are at least partly responsible for the poor defensive rating: turnovers and rebounding.

The Lakers currently rank 27th in the league in turnovers and 27th in opponent points off turnovers. Teams are taking advantage of the Lakers being sloppy.

They also rank 26th in offensive rebounds allowed. Not surprisingly, they rank 29th in opponent second chance points.

Defensively, they're great in lots of areas. They're fifth in steals, fourth in blocks and second in points off turnovers. They're mostly middle of the road or slightly below average in opponent field goal percentage (17th) and opponent 3-point percentage (23rd). And that's not based on shooting luck, as teams are hitting 38.8% of their open threes.

The more this group gains chemistry and familiarity, the better they'll look defensively as well. It's not as simple as fixing those two aspects and being a good defense, but those are the two glaring outliers right now.

The rotations have been pretty ironclad with Luka and Ausint since last year. Luka is going to play the entire first and third quarters and half of the second and fourth quarters to come in at around 36 minutes.

Austin will have his minutes broken up a bit more as he'll leave late in the first quarter, return to open the second, then take another break in the middle of the period before returning to close the frame. Then, he repeats that in the second half.

That has been the rotation for the last two seasons for those two. It ensures that one of them is always on the floor, which is the right approach, and keeps both of them around 36 minutes per game.

When you have two elite on-ball players, you want to give both chances to maximize that skill. Austin is a great off-ball player as well, but giving him his own lineups and time on the floor without Luka, and vice versa, means the Lakers will have a full 48 minutes of an All-Star-level player on the floor.

There will be concerns about both of them after the injuries last season. But the Lakers also need them on the floor as well and 36 minutes per game isn't an unreasonable expectation for a star.

The more likely route for them to get rest is nights off throughout the year. The team already rested Luka once and I imagine that'll happen with some regularity this season.

It's definitely fair to be put off by Quentin Grimes' slow start. Like many, I thought he would get a starting nod to open the season. But perhaps Redick and his staff saw what fans eventually saw when the season started, which was a fair amount of indecisiveness and uncertainty.

The good news is that we are only three weeks into the season and Grimes looked better in the most recent game. It's a matter of him eventually playing more on instinct rather than thinking through the game, which you can tell he's doing right now.

I actually think it'd be better for him, and the team long-term, to be in the starting lineup. It gives that group another creator and dynamic player who can drive or shoot. But it's hard to make the argument to start him based on how he's currently playing.

We're still quite a bit away from being too worried about Grimes. Instead, I'd rather celebrate finding two big wins in Ziaire Williams and Matisse Thybulle so far.

First, I think in this specific play, their judgement was that Thybulle was in an illegal guarding position and committed a foul before any push off occurred. Because there's simply no way to argue there wasn't a push off.

Secondly, as will most things that are a point of emphasis to start the year, I don't suspect it will carry on into the season much. Stars aren't changing how they play to accommodate the rule change. It'll just continue business as usual.

#2800. Steelers Get Major Trade Advice Amid Aaron Rodgers’ Playoff Push

The Pittsburgh Steelers could target a cornerback before the NFL trade deadline as they look to strengthen their secondary for the remainder of the season. With Aaron Rodgers leading the offense, adding a more dependable option in the defensive backfield could help the team maximize its current opportunity to compete.

Pittsburgh's cornerback situation has become an important consideration following the departure of Joey Porter Jr. The Steelers now have Jamel Dean, Asante Samuel Jr. and Daylen Everette available for their starting boundary roles. While the current group could be sufficient to get through the season, the team may benefit from bringing in another experienced defender who can provide greater consistency.

May 28, 2026; Pittsburgh, PA, USA; Pittsburgh Steelers quarterback Aaron Rodgers (8) participates in OTA drills at the UPMC Rooney Sports Complex. Mandatory Credit: Charles LeClaire-Imagn Images

Several potential trade candidates have emerged as options for the Steelers. Riq Woolen of the Philadelphia Eagles, Donte Jackson of the Los Angeles Chargers, Alontae Taylor of the Tennessee Titans, Amik Robertson of the Washington Commanders and Nahshon Wright of the New York Jets are among the players identified as possible targets. Each could give Pittsburgh another option as it evaluates ways to improve its coverage.

The Steelers also have reason to examine their safety depth before the deadline. Strengthening the secondary would give the defense additional flexibility and potentially reduce the pressure on its existing cornerbacks. However, acquiring another player would depend on the availability of suitable targets and whether Pittsburgh believes the potential improvement justifies the cost of a trade.

Jan 12, 2026; Pittsburgh, PA, USA; Pittsburgh Steelers quarterback Aaron Rodgers (8) rolls out during the first half of an AFC Wild Card Round game against the Houston Texans at Acrisure Stadium. Mandatory Credit: Barry Reeger-Imagn Images

Pittsburgh has lined up several players to fill its need for a cornerback. This is detailed in an analysis by Josh Edwards for CBS Sports.

After trading away Joey Porter Jr., Pittsburgh was left with Jamel Dean, Asante Samuel Jr. and Daylen Everette for those starting boundary roles. They may be able to get by this season, but if the plan is to maximize this window with Aaron Rodgers, then they may want more reliable options.

Safety would be another position of interest. Wide receiver has garnered significant discussion but, at some point, the team has to trust the players they have already invested so heavily in.

Potential options: Riq Woolen, Eagles; Donte Jackson, Chargers; Alontae Taylor, Titans; Amik Robertson, Commanders; Nahshon Wright, Jets

Wide receiver has also generated considerable discussion surrounding Pittsburgh's possible NFL deadline moves. The offense could use additional playmaking support, but the organization must consider the investments already made in its current personnel. Rather than continually searching for outside replacements, the Steelers may need to give their existing receivers more opportunities to establish consistency and contribute to the passing attack.

A cornerback addition appears to be a more direct way for Pittsburgh to address a potential weakness as the season progresses. The team does not necessarily need a major overhaul, but another reliable defender could improve its ability to handle opposing passing attacks. With Rodgers' remaining time in the NFL adding urgency to the team's competitive window, the trade deadline presents an opportunity to strengthen the roster without making an unnecessary long-term commitment.

#2797. What makes this 76ers team stacked with superstars so unique

What makes this 76ers team stacked with superstars so unique originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

They're a team of superstars that don't play superstar basketball.

That's the beauty of this 76ers team. They play a brand of unselfish team-first basketball you rarely see from a team loaded with stars.

LeBron James is one of the two or three greatest players in NBA history, a four-time MVP, a four-time NBA champion, a 1st-ballot Hall of Famer.

Joel Embiid is a seven-time all-star, a former MVP, an Olympic gold medalist and a likely Hall of Famer.

Jaylen Brown is a five-time all-star, an NBA champion, a career 20 points-per-game scorer.

Tyrese Maxey is a two-time all-star, one of only two NBA guards averaging 27.0 points per game over the last four years.

Four certified megastars on one team. And somehow they've figured out how to leave their egos at the front door and play smart, tough, disciplined team basketball, the style of ball you just never see on these superstar-laden teams.

On offense, they patiently work the ball around, always looking for the open man or a positive matchup. You've got this bunch of big-name, high-priced All-Stars who are just as happy to pass the ball as shoot it. That never happens.

The 76ers are sixth in the NBA in assists, and they're one of only four teams with twice as many assists as turnovers. Smart, unselfish, patient, disciplined.

When an all-timer like LeBron averages 7.3 assists per game that speaks volumes. LeBron is fifth in the NBA in assists per game among forwards, which is wild. You can see the joy on his face when one of his passes leads to a basket. When one of the greatest players in history has that mentality, it rubs off on everyone around him.

Embiid hasn't really got going yet on offense and still takes too many 3's, but he's also embraced the team-first mentality, and his 4.8 assists per game are more than one full assist above his career average and 4th-most in the NBA among centers or forward-centers. And still one of the most dominating defensive players in the league.

Brown is averaging 27.0 points, which you expect, but he's also averaging 4.3 assists per game, significantly above his career average of 3.8 coming into the season. He's fit in so well so fast you forget its his first season here.

And what can you even say about Maxey? Just a beast. He's averaging nearly 30 points per game but never wastes a possession, always makes the right decision with the ball in his hands and is still finding time to average 4.3 assists per game.

With James, Embiid, Brown and Maxey, the 76ers are the only NBA team with four guys averaging at least 16 points and 4 assists per game. Only the Pacers, Magic and Raptors have three.

But where we're really seeing this group playing out of their minds is on the defensive end, where this bunch of all-stars, including two newcomers, has been astounding.

The 76ers have the 2nd-best defensive efficiency rating in the league, behind only the Pistons, who they play Wednesday night, and they're allowing the 2nd-fewest points in the league, also behind Detroit. Last year, they allowed 123 points per game through eight games. This year they're at 111.

They're holding teams to 43 percent shooting from the field, 3rd-best in the league and their lowest since 2011-12. Their 9.3 steals per game are 6th-most.

All five starters are among the top 25 players in the league in defensive rating – James is fourth, Embiid 11th, Edgecombe 15th, Maxey 22nd and Brown 23rd. How is that even possible?

It's rare to see superstar-caliber guys play as hard on the defensive end as the offensive end. Everybody on this team embraces it. They hustle, they work, they communicate and most importantly they commit to it.

The biggest concern when the 76ers built this dream team was that it would be a team of individuals out for themselves, but the reality has been the opposite.

In a very short period of time, they've learned how to play together. How to play for each other.

And we haven't even mentioned V.J. Edgecomb, who at 21 years old has figured out how to assimilate into this loaded lineup, never force anything, wait for his opportunities and make huge shots when he gets his turn. Edgecombe is at 12.7 points and 3.0 assists per game, and his numbers are understandably down a bit from last year, but if that bothers him it sure doesn't show.

And the role players – Dean Wade, Anfernee Simons, Kentavious Caldwell-Pope, Jabari Walker – have each adopted the same ego-free style of basketball, supporting the starters however they need to.

Nick Nurse deserves a ton of credit for getting this bunch to play this brand of hoops. It's not easy to get any NBA player to put team goals ahead of individual goals. But he's got Hall of Famers and all-stars doing it and having a blast along the way. They all seem to love playing basketball the right way and they seem to love playing together.

The 76ers are 7-1 and looking like a real threat to win the East, and they're doing it by playing the game the right way.

By committing to each other and not themselves.

→ Baca artikel asli

#2796. Mikel Arteta jumps for joy as Arsenal star shines in Champions League win

Arsenal claimed a crucial victory over Slavia Prague, with Viktor Gyökeres playing a pivotal role as the Gunners kept themselves firmly in contention at the UEFA Champions League summit.

The Premier League champions traveled to the Czech capital as heavy favorites, and Noni Madueke wasted little time in breaking the deadlock, netting after just eight minutes. Gyökeres, who had set up the opener, then made his own mark on the scoreline with an outstanding finish.

The second half proved far more challenging for Arsenal, who were forced to hold their nerve during a tense final five minutes after Wiktor Nowak converted from close range to hand Slavia Prague a lifeline.

-Credit:David Price, Arsenal FC via Getty Images

Last season's European runners-up are determined to go all the way this time around, and their victory keeps them just two points adrift of the top two, the only teams boasting perfect records through four matches.

Having already triumphed in Prague twelve months ago, Arsenal set about their business in commanding style. Madueke's blistering pace allowed him to latch onto a Gyökeres through ball, and the winger kept his composure to open the scoring inside the first ten minutes, as per The Mirror.

Despite controlling possession, Arsenal didn't bombard the Slavia Prague goal, but they did extend their advantage on 37 minutes through a moment of real quality from Gyökeres. The Swede, who still has his share of critics, combined slickly with Mikel Merino before opening up his body to finish with conviction, aided by a fortunate ricochet off the post.

The hosts kicked off the second period with fresh vigor and Oskar Kubiak's long-range attempt forced David Raya into one of his rare interventions of the evening. Arsenal did ultimately regain dominance, but with the Czech club presenting such minimal danger, the urgency for a third strike was largely absent.

Slavia Prague had penalty claims involving Mikel Merino dismissed with 15 minutes left on the clock, but finally managed to give their supporters a moment to celebrate during stoppage time when Nowak converted from a David Jurásek delivery, though it would merely serve as a consolation.

To listen to the latest episode of 'All Out Soccer' - CLICK HERE

An assist and a goal in the opening half provided the ideal foundation for the Arsenal forward who, seemingly with every appearance, must answer his doubters. The verdict remains undecided on whether the Swede will become the regular spearhead of the Gunners' attack.

However, on Wednesday evening the Swede was firing on all cylinders, and Arteta was leaping with delight when he witnessed Gyökeres extend Arsenal's advantage, with his belief in his forward remaining steadfast. The Arsenal manager has consistently offered public support for the ex-Sporting Lisbon man, and seldom has he marked a second strike in a fairly straightforward match with such enthusiasm.

Gyökeres has consistently appeared to be a player who thrives on belief, and Wednesday's performance will have benefited him greatly as he continues competing with Kai Havertz. The pair have alternated in spearheading the Arsenal offense, each contributing distinctly different attributes.

The Gunners splashed significant funds on Gyökeres, who was yet to prove himself in a major European league before Arteta came calling. They naturally expect a return on that outlay and the Arsenal manager is clearly happy to stick with him. It's not as though we haven't witnessed the quality he possesses — the frustration is how often we see it.

-Credit:David Price, Arsenal FC via Getty Images

No team has played more games in the Champions League than Arsenal without winning it. Yet all indicators suggest the North Londoners are better equipped than at any previous point to bring that wait to an end.

Countless clubs have suffered final defeats before claiming the title the following season — Liverpool in 2019 being the most recent example. Arsenal will look to follow suit, and they have shown consistent growth in this competition year after year under Arteta.

Three seasons ago Bayern Munich eliminated them in the quarterfinals, the next year brought semifinal heartbreak against Paris Saint-Germain, and last season's defeat to the French giants once again is well documented.

It does appear, however, that this Arsenal side is discovering what it takes to thrive in the Champions League with each passing campaign. They were thoroughly impressive in the league phase last term, posting a perfect record, but countless teams have demonstrated that peaking after the New Year serves you far better than hitting form before it.

• Aston Villa send clear message to Manchester United over Unai Emery's future

• Ex-England star on Gilberto Mora transfer battle as Liverpool and Arsenal linked

Slavia Prague etched their name into the record books on Wednesday evening, though not in the manner they hoped, as their winless streak stretched to 23 matches. That tally includes eight draws, but the bulk are naturally losses.

Their defeat at the hands of Arsenal means they have now gone 23 Champions League fixtures without victory since they secured a win in their inaugural appearance in the tournament back in 2007.

The setback has now matched the longest winless sequence in European Cup history, equaling the forgettable stretches posted by both AEK Athens (1989-2006) and Steaua Bucharest (2006-2013).

Supporters who packed into the Fortuna Arena paid handsomely for the privilege of witnessing the Czechs establish unwanted history. The Premier League champions are naturally a major attraction and, once converted, the home club was demanding upwards of £120 ($162) to watch Declan Rice, Gabriel Magalhães and company strut their stuff.

#2794. Chargers open 21-day practice window for LB Denzel Perryman

The Chargers opened the 21-day practice window for tight end David Njoku and linebacker Denzel Perryman, coach Jim Harbaugh announced Wednesday.

Harbaugh said on Monday that Njoku was ready to return from injured reserve.

Njoku, 30, went on injured reserve after injuring his fibula in Week 2 and missed the past five games. He caught six of eight targets for 67 yards and no touchdowns before his injury.

The Chargers have leaned on Oronde Gadsden and Charlie Kolar in Njoku’s absence.

In his 10th season, Njoku has totaled 390 catches for 4,129 yards and 34 touchdowns.

Perryman went on injured reserve before the season with an elbow ligament tear, and he has missed seven games.

Perryman, 33, played only 10 games in 2025 due to a high-ankle sprain and a suspension.

#2792. Observations after Sixers beat Bucks to move to 3-0, Embiid posts triple-double

Observations after Sixers beat Bucks to move to 3-0, Embiid posts triple-double originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Sixers stayed unbeaten Saturday night at Xfinity Mobile Arena, earning a 127-116 win over the Bucks and improving to 3-0 on the season.

Joel Embiid had his 10th career regular-season triple-double, tallying 28 points, 11 rebounds and 10 assists.

On his 30th birthday, Jaylen Brown posted 27 points and five assists. Tyrese Maxey had 26 points. VJ Edgecombe scored 17 and grabbed 13 boards.

The Sixers' absences on the first night of a back-to-back included LeBron James (low back/sciatica management). Sixers head coach Nick Nurse said pregame that he did not know yet who would be available for his team's matchup with the Pistons on Sunday.

Here are observations on the Sixers' victory Saturday:

Dean Wade made his Sixers debut and started in James' place. He'd missed the first two games of the regular season with a left ankle sprain.

The 3-and-D forward scored the night's first points by a sinking a corner three-pointer on the opening possession.

Embiid also had a sharp start. He moved well, made good decisions and served as the main hub of his team's offense in the early going.

The Sixers' seven-time All-Star center has often enjoyed facing Myles Turner. He entered the night with an average of 29.4 points in 18 previous games against the Bucks big man.

Embiid did a little bit of everything in the first quarter, posting eight points, four assists and four rebounds over nine minutes. After snatching an offensive board, Embiid drained a fadeaway jumper to give the Sixers a 10-1 lead.

One of Embiid's assists was quite stylish. He found Edgecombe open beyond the arc with a behind-the-back bounce pass. Edgecombe knocked down the shot and wound up going 3 for 4 from three-point range in the first quarter.

The Sixers added rookie Labaron Philon Jr. to their rotation. Jabari Walker stayed in the mix, though he didn't enter until the 7:36 mark of the second quarter.

Philon played four minutes in his NBA debut and missed his lone shot attempt, a corner three. On defense, Philon allowed a couple of blow-bys off the dribble. He generally looked like he belonged on offense, albeit without any production in his brief run.

Maxey banked in a half-court heave at the first-quarter buzzer to put the Sixers up nine points. The Sixers couldn't carry forward any momentum, however, and began the second quarter poorly.

Adem Bona had another rough stint amid a subpar start to the season. After Anfernee Simons missed a transition layup try, Ryan Rollins' three lifted the Bucks into a 40-37 lead.

The dreadful stretch for the Sixers on both sides of the ball called to mind how they'd fallen into a 29-point deficit Thursday night vs. the Cavaliers before clawing out a comeback win.

The Sixers mustered just seven points in first six minutes of the second quarter and had many stagnant possessions that concluded with contested jumpers. The team's transition defense was dismal for much of the second period. Milwaukee had the game's first 19 fast-break points.

The Sixers regained the lead late in the second quarter. Kentavious Caldwell-Pope made two three-balls in the second and nabbed three first-half steals. Embiid assisted a go-ahead, and-one Walker layup.

Brown hit a three with 11.8 seconds left in the second quarter. Unsurprisingly, his variety of shotmaking skills were useful without James in the lineup.

Even when James or Embiid sit, the Sixers should have a simple advantage in terms of volume of stars over most opponents this year. None of Brown, Embiid or Maxey had the sort of off night that would have improved the Bucks' odds of a road win. They'd all passed the 20-point mark by the end of the third quarter.

Maxey notched another final-possession bucket, this time with a more conventional make. He canned a jumper from the right elbow with 1.7 seconds left in the third to extend the Sixers' lead to 97-87.

The Bucks made a late push in the fourth quarter. Kel'el Ware's put-back, and-one layup cut the Milwaukee's deficit to 117-114.

The Sixers ultimately handled business. Embiid reached his triple-double by assisting a second-chance Brown jumper and and Caldwell-Pope soon drilled a dagger corner three. Embiid even threw down a last-minute dunk for good measure.

For the second consecutive season, the Sixers have a 3-0 start. They got to four wins last year before dropping their first game.

#2789. UPDATED: Chicago Bears 16-Man Practice Squad

The Chicago Bears have a 16-man practice squad, but the list will be fluid, and we'll update it throughout the year.

UPDATE (11/4): The Bears signed TE Stephen Carlson to the active roster and signed DE Earnest Brown IV to the practice squad.

Practice squad injured: Anthony Johnson Jr. (S)

Here are the qualifications for practice squad eligibility and some rules to remember.

A 17th player is permitted as long as he qualifies for the NFL's International Pathway Program.

Players with no accrued seasons (rookies) and those with fewer than nine regular-season games in their lone accrued season.

A maximum of ten players are allowed who have earned no more than two accrued seasons.

Up to six players with unlimited accrued seasons are permitted on the practice squad.

However, the number of players in the last two categories cannot exceed 10 total players.

Teams can elevate up to two practice squad players for each game, but they can still only have 48 players active on game day. After the game, the elevated player(s) will revert to the practice squad. A player can be elevated up to three times, but if he's needed after that, the team would be required to sign him to the 53-man active roster.

Players on practice squads are free agents who can be signed to another team's active roster. A player's current club can't block him from leaving; they can only make a counteroffer to keep him. Ultimately, it's the player's decision. If a player is signed away, his new team must keep him on its 53-man roster for three weeks, but if he's released, he'll revert to his previous team. Players signed to another active roster are also guaranteed three weeks' salary.

A practice squad player can't sign with his team's upcoming opponent unless he does so six days before the upcoming game or ten days prior if his team is on its bye week.

#2785. LeBron Log No. 3: The King dishes, Caitlin Clark visits and closing time

LeBron Log No. 3: The King dishes, Caitlin Clark visits and closing time originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

LeBron James' latest week with the Sixers included his first 10-assist game with Philadelphia, his best performance with the team, a visit with Caitlin Clark, a crowd-wide finger wag and one jumper he would probably like back.

Some of it belonged in the box score. The rest quickly made its way around social media.

Welcome to the third edition of the LeBron Log.

Throughout the preseason, the Sixers discussed the responsibility that accompanied their desire to play faster.

They wanted to advance the ball before defenses could organize, but Nick Nurse was not interested in accepting extra turnovers as part of the tradeoff. When asked whether he could live with more mistakes if they came with better pace, his answer was simple.

Seven games into the regular season, the Sixers have not yet become one of the NBA's fastest teams. They entered Monday ranked 20th in pace.

Philadelphia has committed an NBA-low 12.7 turnovers per game. James has helped organize the offense as its leading facilitator, recording 35 assists against 11 turnovers.

He leads the Sixers in total assists despite playing only five games. Tyrese Maxey, who has appeared in all seven, is second with 31.

James provided the cleanest example Wednesday night against the Pacers. He finished with 13 points, 10 assists, five rebounds, two blocks and no turnovers during the Sixers' 114-109 comeback victory.

It was his first double-digit-assist performance with Philadelphia. According to StatMuse, James also became the oldest player in its database to record at least 10 points and 10 assists while committing no more than one turnover.

James' night was not flashy by his standards. He attempted 12 shots and finished fifth on the team in scoring. Still, he helped five Sixers reach double figures without surrendering a possession.

The Sixers signed the NBA's all-time leading scorer. Through the opening weeks of his Philadelphia career, he has been their leading passer.

James entered his first NBA Cup game with the Sixers possessing more tournament history than anyone else on the roster.

He led the Lakers to the inaugural Cup championship in 2023 and was named the event's first MVP after averaging 26.4 points, eight rebounds and seven assists during Los Angeles' unbeaten run.

Three years later, his first Cup game in red, white and blue produced his best Sixers performance yet.

James recorded 20 points, nine rebounds, eight assists, one steal and one block in Friday's 98-93 win over the Knicks. He shot 8 for 15 and saved two of his most important baskets for the final 70 seconds.

Jalen Brunson's three-pointer gave New York a 90-89 lead with 1:32 remaining. On the ensuing possession, James backed down OG Anunoby, turned toward the middle and sank a fadeaway to move the Sixers back in front.

VJ Edgecombe extended the lead with a three-pointer as the shot clock expired. After Brunson made two free throws, Maxey drove for a layup and missed. James followed the play, collected the offensive rebound and scored the putback with 11.9 seconds remaining.

The basket sealed Philadelphia's second win over the defending champions and its first of NBA Cup group play.

"Best game all around," Nurse said. "He's been good, but that was his best game for sure."

James has played more than 1,600 regular-season games, but his Philadelphia closing reel has officially begun.

The fourth-quarter baskets were not James' only memorable moments Friday.

After converting an and-one earlier in the game, James broke out his familiar shoulder-shrug celebration. Edgecombe joined him, smiling as he mirrored the move alongside his 41-year-old teammate.

The pair had already developed a personalized handshake featuring two taps to the head and a forceful shrug. This one came during the game.

James supplied another recognizable celebration in the third quarter.

Anunoby drove toward the basket with James trailing the play. James caught him near the rim and was officially credited with his fifth block of the season.

He turned toward the crowd and wagged his finger. The fans joined in.

Edgecombe later delivered the biggest shot of the game, and James met him with an embrace near the sideline. The two are separated by more than 20 years, but they have quickly developed a collection of celebrations.

"He doesn't shy away from the moment," James said of the 21-year-old. "Young kid, but he thrives in the moment."

James was not the only basketball attraction inside Gainbridge Fieldhouse on Wednesday.

Indiana Fever star Caitlin Clark watched the Sixers' win from courtside and brought a small camera with her. It was the second time this year she photographed James during a game.

In March, Clark served as a credentialed photographer when James and the Lakers visited Indiana.

"The KING 👑 … Shot by ME," Clark wrote when she shared those photos.

After Wednesday's game, Clark made her way toward the visiting locker room. She briefly met Maxey before speaking with James in the hallway.

James gave Clark one of his No. 23 Sixers jerseys. The team shared photos of the exchange with a fitting caption.

Clark publicly expressed hope that James would join the Pacers before he signed with Philadelphia. Their mutual respect goes back further.

During Clark's first WNBA season in 2024, James compared the attention surrounding her professional debut to what he experienced when he entered the NBA directly from high school.

"I'm rooting for Caitlin because I've been in that seat before," James said at the time. "I've walked that road before."

James did not speak with reporters after Wednesday's game, so he was not asked about their conversation.

Maxey was asked about meeting Clark for the first time.

"That's just another person that I like to watch," he said. "She cold. She nice. Seeing her today was really cool. Sometimes you try not to fan out, but it was really cool seeing her. It's her building; she plays here too."

James and Edgecombe also produced the week's most widely shared Sixers warmup clip.

Before Wednesday's game, the two danced together near the three-point line. Edgecombe moved away while James stepped back and launched a jumper.

The clip spread quickly across social media. James still walked out of downtown Indianapolis with 10 assists, no turnovers and a victory.

James has made more field goals than anyone in NBA history. Even the career scoring leader is entitled to one warmup mulligan.
