#2776. Observations after Sixers beat Bulls to reach East-leading 7-1

Observations after Sixers beat Bulls to reach East-leading 7-1 originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Sixers kept up their hot start to the season Monday night at Xfinity Mobile Arena, reaching 7-1 with a 126-107 win against the Bulls.

All five of the Sixers' starters scored in double figures. Tyrese Maxey posted 26 points on 10-for-15 shooting and 11 assists. Jaylen Brown tallied a season-best 25 points and 11 rebounds.

VJ Edgecombe's 21 points were a new season high. LeBron James recorded 15 points, nine assists and seven boards.

Bulls rookie Caleb Wilson had 24 points and seven rebounds.

Joel Embiid (15 points, eight rebounds) had been listed as questionable with an official designation of "right foot plantar fascia injury management." He sat out Saturday on the second night of a back-to-back for the Sixers' win over the Hornets.

"Plantar is a little bit of a come and go type of thing," Sixers head coach Nick Nurse told reporters pregame. "They're just trying to manage it. It's very painful, if you've ever had it. It can kind of come and go out of nowhere. So we're just trying to manage the pain with that, be as smart as we can."

Nurse told reporters that Dominick Barlow is set to see a specialist Tuesday about the back injury that's sidelined him to start the season.

"He's been on court a little bit … not a ton of moving around," Nurse said. "I think they want to get to tomorrow's appointment. Hopefully, it'll be positive and we can get into his workouts a little bit."

The Sixers will begin a three-game road trip Wednesday when they visit the Pistons. Here are observations on their victory Monday:

After Embiid blitzed a Bulls pick-and-roll, Maxey picked off Matas Buzelis' hurried pass to get the Sixers rolling in transition and James jammed home the team's first field goal.

Edgecombe got a slam soon after James grabbed a defensive rebound and the Sixers again pushed the pace. The Sixers scored six quick fast-break points, leading the Bulls to burn an early timeout, and James' long-distance feeds were a major asset.

"If you're open, he's going to pass it," Edgecombe said of James. "The main thing is just being ready. Tonight I had to run and go get 'em. If you're open and he sees you, he's going to pass the ball."

Edgecombe had a dunk-heavy first quarter. He threw down his second when Dean Wade nabbed a steal and dropped off a typically selfless dish to the Sixers' explosive second-year guard, whose slam put the team up 22-13.

The Sixers have an abundance of dangerous options in the open floor, Edgecombe included. As he's already shown with his continued clutch shotmaking, the 21-year-old won't become anything resembling a non-factor offensively this year as a byproduct of the Sixers' enhanced star power.

Over the team's first seven games, Edgecombe had averaged 11.3 points. He scored 11 in the first period on 5-for-5 shooting.

Adem Bona missed a dunk early in the second quarter, which stung a bit more when Jalen Smith drilled a three-pointer seconds later.

Bona remained the Sixers' main backup center. He had a poor first-half stint overall amid a largely negative start to his third season. The 23-year-old big man played well in the Sixers' victory over the Bucks, posting six points, 13 rebounds and three blocks. Outside of that, his season has been sparse on bright moments so far. It's reasonable to wonder when Nurse might turn to alternatives like Jabari Walker, who played a good game Saturday in Charlotte, or try James again at the five.

The Bulls outscored the Sixers by eight points in Bona's first run, cutting their derficit to 40-38. Chicago went in front with a three by former Sixer Buddy Hield.

The Sixers regained the advantage late in the second quarter. Maxey and James each were excellent as passers in the first half, combining for 14 assists and three turnovers. James fired the ball to Wade for a corner three that gave the Sixers a 60-52 lead.

Following a third-quarter Embiid fadeaway jumper and two James lay-ins, the Sixers were up 17 points.

The Bulls cut the Sixers' lead down to 11 points by the start of the fourth quarter. With Bona still struggling and on four fouls, Wade began the fourth at center. Bona then briefly spelled Wade before being whistled for his fifth foul and taking a seat. Embiid ultimately wasn't needed for any of the fourth period and only logged 25 minutes.

Embiid said postgame that he was not available to return to the action in the fourth and has been feeling his plantar fasciitis on the court. The seven-time All-Star's health is clearly not close to 100 percent.

"I was on a minutes restriction," he said. "(The foot) has been going like that for the past few weeks. It's painful. Just got to manage it.

"If it flares up pretty bad, got to take a step back. We've been able to manage it, but it has its ups and downs."

The Sixers' lead got as low as eight points, but Chicago did not shoot well enough from three-point range (26.3 percent) or have sufficient firepower to sustain a meaningful comeback run. The bottom line is the Sixers are in a different class than the Bulls, who fell to 1-6.

As for the Sixers, they'll wake up Tuesday morning at the top of the Eastern Conference standings.

"You've got some of these nights where you kind of call them professional wins. …You look at the records and all that kind of stuff, but they have a lot of talent," Nurse said. "A lot of these teams play a way that's a little scary. It's fast and it's furious, lots of threes. … They're going to keep coming at you, high-possession games. So they're not easy games and I'm glad that we handled it professionally tonight."

#2774. Rich Paul Debates LeBron James’ Impact on Lakers’ 2026-27 Season

Support Local Media - Click to Add LA Sports Report as a Preferred Source on Google

The new-look Los Angeles Lakers are off to a flying start without LeBron James, and Max Kellerman argued the NBA's All-Time leading scorer's departure lit a fire under their current stars.

James left the Lakers in the offseason, joining the Philadelphia 76ers and headlining a busy offseason for the Eastern Conference squad. So far, though, the Lakers don't appear to be missing him. They currently have a 6-2 record on the year, and Luka Doncic leads the entire NBA in scoring with 38.3 points per game as well as 8.0 rebounds and 8.1 assists so far on the season.

"I think Luka was very motivated once LeBron got moved," Kellerman said. "…The whole was not greater than some of its parts with those two on the floor. Part of that happens when a guy needs the ball. The specter of LeBron, and Luka trying to do the right thing, LeBron trying to do the right thing, created a confusion on the team."

In response, James' agent Rich Paul argued the Lakers' start had nothing to do with LeBron leaving.

"What does LeBron have to do with it? LeBron doesn't have anything to do with that," Paul said. "…I think as media, we have to get out of the habit of mentioning LeBron. That man doesn't have anything to do with this year."

While the Lakers certainly look good thus far without James on their roster, comparing this year's Lakers with last year's team isn't exactly fair.

To start, the Lakers didn't just lose James and roll out the same team. They made several moves during the offseason to strengthen weak areas on their roster and fill the hole James left.

Walker Kessler joined the team in the offseason, filling the hole the Lakers had at center seemingly since the departure of Anthony Davis, and he is off to a great start with 14.4 points and 10.5 rebounds per game. Alongside Kessler, the Lakers also added defensive pieces like Ziaire Williams and Matisse Thybulle after struggling defensively last season.

Additionally, the Lakers have only played eight games so far this season, which is far too small of a sample size to compare to their full season with both Doncic and James last year. It's far too soon to compare the two Lakers teams, and their success will be graded by their future rather than their present.

#2772. Todd Monken: Deshaun Watson not ruled out yet, but Shedeur Sanders preparing to start

Browns quarterback Deshaun Watson is in the concussion protocol after self-reporting symptoms on Tuesday.

Head coach Todd Monken told reporters in his Wednesday press conference that Watson hasn’t necessarily been ruled out for Sunday’s game against New Orleans. But the club is preparing Shedeur Sanders to start.

“What I would say is, credit to Deshaun — after the game, [he] felt fine. Monday night, started feeling a little bit, little bit of a headache, and came in Tuesday and, you know, reported,” Monken said, via transcript from the team. “So, credit to him for someone who really loves playing the game. And so, he is in the protocol. And Shedeur will take the reps today with the ones.”

Monken admitted that it’s “a little more difficult” for a quarterback to play on Sunday after missing practice due to a concussion. But Monken and the team will let the process play out.

“I mean, there’s a long time between now and then, right? I mean, look where the Ravens are at. And you don’t know as the week goes on what could happen,” Monken said. “It’s not ideal, right? It’s not ideal for any player to go through a week and not practice and then play. We know that. But every situation is different.”

Monken noted that Watson can participate in the team’s meetings to help him stay prepared in the event that he gets cleared. The team has been game-planning with the idea that Sanders will start. But Monken said the game plan won’t change much if it’s Sanders or Watson behind center.

“I don’t see the way [it would change], because the rest of the guys are the same, and they’re similar in some ways,” Monken said. “Some things, you know, Shedeur’s skill set is different than Deshaun’s, but still very similar in terms of what we do. We can’t change that dramatically.”

Either way, Watson is expected to still be the starter when he is cleared.

“Yeah, I didn’t assume I’d get that question. Yeah, he’s been our starter for eight games. I would assume he’s still our starter,” Monken said. “I don’t — I’m thinking about Wednesday. But I’m not — I’m really thinking about today and watching Shedeur play today.”

So far this season, Watson has completed 65.5 percent of his passes for 1,806 yards with seven touchdowns and three interceptions. He’s been sacked a league-high 33 times. His eight games this season are the most he’s played in a year for the Browns since arriving on the club in 2022.

Last season, Sanders completed 56.6 percent of his throws for 1,400 yards with seven touchdowns and 10 interceptions in eight games with seven starts.

#2770. ‘I'm finding my footing': Jaylen Brown's role taking shape with Sixers

'I'm finding my footing': Jaylen Brown's role taking shape with Sixers originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

Jaylen Brown has spent the opening weeks of the season describing the Sixers as a work in progress.

He offered a similarly humble perspective Monday night at Xfinity Mobile Arena. His performance, however, provided the clearest look yet at how he can influence this group while sharing the floor with the club's other established stars.

Brown finished with 25 points, 11 rebounds and five assists in the Sixers' 126-107 win over the Chicago Bulls. He shot 9 for 15 from the field, made four three-pointers and did not commit a turnover.

Five of his rebounds came on the offensive end, an area Brown has emphasized while the Sixers continue piecing together their frontcourt rotation.

"Just a mindset," Brown said. "Just trying to create extra opportunities for my team. As we're trying to patch together our fives and our front line, I try to make an extra contribution and emphasis on the glass."

Brown has also been careful about how he describes his place on a roster that already belonged to Joel Embiid and Tyrese Maxey.

Maxey scored 15 of his 26 points in the fourth quarter Monday, continuing his torrid start to the season. He has led the Sixers in scoring while being the only starter to appear in all eight games.

Brown is not interested in interrupting that.

"Maxey's been leading the way, 100 percent," Brown said. "He's been here. This is his team. Him and Joel have been here for a while, so the mindset has been to complement those guys. I'm finding my footing, but Maxey has been leading the way for sure."

Brown's footing looked firm against Chicago.

Beyond his scoring, he helped the Sixers control the glass, moved the ball within an offense that generated 23 assists during the first half and supplied the physicality Nick Nurse expects from him.

"The dude is tough and he wins," Nurse said. "I'll take that all day long. The degree of difficulty on some of the shots he hit was really amazing. I thought he pounded the glass and got some key rebounds. Makes a lot of winning plays. Man, he's a winner."

Brown spent the first 10 years of his career in Boston. He won a championship there, reached another NBA Finals and grew from a 20-year-old prospect into one of the league's premier two-way wings.

Moving into a new system alongside James, Embiid and Maxey was never going to be seamless.

"I mean, he's fitting in along with all of us," James said. "We're getting better and better. It's not going to happen overnight. He spent his first 10 years in a system in Boston, in the city, so it's going to take some adjustment.

"It takes adjustment for him. It's going to take adjustment for the rest of the guys that joined this franchise as well. But tonight was a great step."

James pointed to Brown's "pressure, his shot-making ability, his rebounding ability" and his professionalism.

Brown's early influence has extended beyond his production.

The Sixers entered Monday with one of the NBA's best half-court defenses. Brown has been one of the group's most persistent voices, stressing communication, preparation and attention to opposing players' tendencies.

"That's the part that I care about the most as a group, not individually," Brown said. "Having a mentality night in and night out, respecting your opponent, respecting the game, making sure that we are executing our coverages, we're being clear in our communication and then we're also taking away tendencies on a night-to-night basis.

"Those things I just explained are over 50 percent of the battle."

Brown understands that maintaining those details across an 82-game schedule will require more than reminders.

"A little bit of accountability," he said. "You learn different guys require different things. How you communicate with each guy may be a little different, but accountability for sure and confrontation if need be.

"The offense is going to be what it's going to be, but our defense has got to be non-negotiable. That's something I say all the time. Non-negotiable. You want to be a good team, defense is non-negotiable."

The Sixers showed both sides of their defensive potential Monday.

They built a double-digit lead during the first half, allowed Chicago to score 36 points in the second quarter and then created separation after halftime. Philadelphia held the Bulls to 20 points in the third quarter before pulling away in the fourth.

Brown did not celebrate their ability to raise their intensity after it slipped.

"I don't like that we have that," he said. "It's good that we have shown the potential, but we've got to keep that switch on. It doesn't work like that where you just flip the switch.

"It can catch up to us and it can bite us against a better team or on a night that we're not being as crisp on offense. It can't be a switch just because we're playing a lesser opponent or whatever the case may be. We've got to make sure that mentality is from the beginning to end. Can't skip any steps."

That urgency accompanies considerable optimism.

Brown said the Sixers enjoy playing together. He loves when James grabs a rebound because "he's like a quarterback," capable of launching an outlet pass or immediately pushing the pace.

The group is learning when to attack, when to move the ball and how to avoid hesitating in its effort to play unselfishly.

"It's a lot of talent, man," Brown said. "We like each other, and we're just kind of building that chemistry of how we can get this thing to a well-oiled machine. I think we're taking steps forward every single day."

The Sixers are 7-1 despite Brown believing those steps remain incomplete. They have won close games, erased sizable deficits and received important contributions from different players.

Brown's performance Monday showed another version of how their pieces can fit.

"We still have a long way to go to being a really, really solid, great team," Brown said. "But we've been able to win some games. We started out the season great, 7-1, and you haven't seen our best version yet.

#2768. Jay volleyball's historic season ends with state championship

Jay volleyball's march to history is complete.

The Royals are state champions for the first time in school history after beating Branford in five sets (25-22, 22-25, 25-22, 13-25, 15-8) to win the Rural state championship.

"We still feel like we're on cloud nine," Jay head coach Carey Locklin told the PNJ In a phone interview after the match. "We still feel like we're in a dream, and it's a really good dream."

Jay senior Darby Godwin was a monster in her final high school game, finishing with 26 kills on a .333 hitting percentage and 21 digs. Aubreigh Nelson also finished with a double of 14 kills and 19 digs as did libero Lanie Stephens with 34 digs and 10 assists and setter Bailey Lowry with 44 assists and 16 digs. Kylei Martinez totaled five kills and 18 digs, while Abigail Persichini added six kills. They hit .210 as a team.

For Branford, senior Anabella Dicks left it all on the court with 31 kills and 24 digs. Paisley Knight finished with 13 kills and Ava Alcorn a match-high 57 digs. The Buccaneers hit .222 as a team.

The match was played in front of a raucous crowd at the Polk State College's Winter Haven Health Center, as Branford chartered a spirit bus full of students to make the trip south. A Jay parent ordered noisemakers on Amazon last night, Locklin said, and she told her players to imagine that Buccaneer blue was also Jay blue.

Jay (28-2) trailed by at least four points in the first four sets, yet still managed to turn things around in the first and third set to stay afloat before Branford (29-3) steamrolled through the fourth set, where Locklin said it looked like her team forgot how to play volleyball.

The defending Rural state champions who were also making their fourth straight state championship appearance, led 13-9 before Godwin and Nelson helped flip the script. Jay went on an 11-2 run to take a 20-15 lead and hold the Buccaneers late charge.

Branford then led 17-12 in the second set before Jay battled back to cut the lead to 20-19, but Dicks and the Buccaneers found enough holes in the Royals defense to tie the match. They parlayed that into a 12-5 lead to start the third set before Jay chipped away. Branford still led 20-16 in the third set, before the Royals closed on a 9-2 run, with Nelson getting three kills in the final four points.

The Buccaneers stormed back with their season on the line, leading 13-3 in the fourth set and never sweating. Branford was 4-0 in five set matches entering today, while Jay lost it's lone five set match against T.R. Miller on Aug. 27.

But Jay had come too far to fall short of its mission and sprinted ahead with a state championship hanging in the balance. They led 4-1, then after Branford rallied for a 5-5 tie scored six straight points and were never threatened again. Godwin clinched the championship with a push to the back, catching Branford on its heals, with the Buccaneers failing to return the free ball.

"She's just a phenomenal athlete," Locklin said of Godwin. "She's been up and ready to play since seven o'clock this morning. She's locked in and serious. There ain't a lot of playing around with Darby. She's all business...I told her in that fifth set if they start shutting you down then I'll move you to middle or opposite side and she said 'I got this, I'm good. They're not shutting me down.'"

The title continues Jay athletics impressive run in the last five years. The Royals won a softball state championship in 2022, a baseball state championship in 2024 and played for the girls basketball state championship in February.

In the Jay high school gym, pictures of the 1995 and 1996 girls basketball state championship teams hang on the near wall. Soon enough, the 2026 Jay volleyball team will join them.

"Several of these last 10 years...it was oh we were really close to winning a district championship," Locklin said. "Then it was oh we almost won the regional and we missed it. Last year winning the district and getting thrown out in the regional fired us up like 'we're going to win the district again' and this time we're going to put a regional championship under our belt. Once we got that we were like 'oh let's go.'"

"I think it sets a precedent for our volleyball program at Jay High School about what can happen with a lot of hard work. But it takes years. There's building years, that's what coaches do. You get a group for four years and you try to get the best you can out of them.

This article originally appeared on Pensacola News Journal: Jay volleyball's historic season ends with state championship

#2765. LeBron James is having fun in Philly and one fan's wine can prove it

LeBron James is having fun in Philly and one fan's wine can prove it originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

LeBron James is one of kind in so many ways. What he did Monday night might be the cherry on top.

In the third quarter of the Sixers' 126-107 victory over the Bulls, James decided he'd take fan interaction to a whole new level.

James looked for a transition pass from Tyrese Maxey, who snagged a rebound. James was behind the pass and his momentum took him into the stands, where he disappeared for a few moments.

It wasn't until another angle was shown on replay that we could see what actually went down in the darkness of the seats. James didn't hold back giving reporters a play-by-play.

"I spilled some of his wine," he said postgame. "I crashed into him, spilled some of his wine. So I wanted to see if it was worth it on my arm and how good it was. It was alright."

Troy Clemons – the Sixers fan whose drink spilled – explained his perspective on the interaction with James to Kris Rhim of ESPN.

"[I was] walking to my seat … LeBron crashed into me, he grabbed me, he said 'My bad,' my drink spilled everywhere. He took my drink from me and he basically just took a sip of it, gave it back and ran back on the [court]."

"At the end of the day he's another man like me," Clemons said. "I mean he's LeBron, but at the end of the day, I threw that cup in the trash. And it's nothing up with him, I just got a fresh drink though."

It should be noted that players are not supposed to drink from fans' cups, but it's rare for the NBA to fine someone for a spontaneous interaction such as this. In 2019, a similar thing happened when Mike Scott tumbled into the Bucks' court-side seats. The Sixers star jokingly took a sip of a fan's beverage and the NBA opted not to fine him. Time will tell if James has the same fate.

If nothing else, an interaction like this just shows how much fun James seems to be having.

The 24-year veteran noted the importance of finding joy in the game as he grinds through the season.

"It's a long marathon. And if you don't find ways to have fun throughout it, even though you're working and even though you're locked in, then it could wear on you. So for me, especially at this point in my career, being able to just appreciate the moments, the small moments, these fans are great to us. We try to be great to them as well. By playing the game the right way, playing the game hard and then also just being showmen as well, it was a great game for us."

Head coach Nick Nurse also weighed in on the 41-year-old's mindset coming into the season.

"This summer I think he probably stated publicly that he wanted to go some place and have some fun playing. He loves the game, [it's his] 24th year. It's a group of guys, like I keep saying, that have been super focused in the intent of playing the right way. I think he's a big part of that."

The vibes are high. It's difficult for them not to be.

With Monday's win against the Bulls, the Sixers are 7-1 and lead the Eastern Conference. They've won four in a row and the only team they've lost to is the Pistons. The Sixers will face them on Wednesday to start a three-game road trip.

However, they have him listed as probable to play on Wednesday due to "lower body injury management." It's a strange designation that doesn't tell us much, but the positive part is the probable tag. Keep an eye on this situation, but he isn't expected to miss any time.

He should be rostered until Leonard returns, and he still may hold value beyond that. Walter has been effective on both ends of the ball and continues to be one of the team's best shooters.

Chaney Johnson has filled in as a starter, and while he has been solid, he hasn't done much for fantasy managers. Nolan Traore has been the one to step up with Dëmin sidelined, and the second-year guard has taken advantage of the opportunity. Traore is an intriguing add, and he should continue to start even after Dëmin returns.

Watson missed Monday's game against the Thunder with a hamstring injury and is set to remain out in Wednesday's rematch. Meleek Thomas got the starting nod in place of Watson, with Mario Hezonja and Sam Merrill joining Thomas in playing 26 minutes. All three should continue to see a boost until Watson or Jaylon Tyson returns. However, Thomas should be the priority waiver wire add, assuming he continues to start. He has been impressive this season, especially for a second-round pick on a playoff team.

Tyson is set to miss a third straight game with an ankle injury on Wednesday. He would have had the opportunity to start with Watson sidelined, but that role has been passed to Thomas. There isn't much clarity as to when Watson or Tyson will return to the lineup, but they will both remain out against OKC on Wednesday. They play the 76ers on Friday, which would be the next time either Watson or Tyson can play.

The Clippers assigned Ingram (heel), Yanic Konan Niederhäuser (foot) and Max Strus (foot) to the G League on Sunday to continue their recovery processes and recalled all three on Monday. Strus ended up making his season debut on Monday, while BI and YKN remained out. There isn't a specific timeline as to when Ingram could play again, but this is a positive step for him. Hopefully it won't be much longer.

#2760. Power-hitter Lee Ivakina continues to be key to UAA volleyball's success as a senior

Nov. 4—Since arriving on campus two years ago as a junior college transfer, Lee Ivakina has been integral to the University of Alaska Anchorage volleyball team's ability to thrive on the court.

In her first season with the team, she led the Seawolves in kills and points and finished third in digs, and she is on track to repeat all three feats heading into the final two weeks of the 2026 regular season.

Through 24 matches played, Ivakina is the only player on the team with 300-plus points and kills. She is averaging career highs with 3.48 kills and 4.03 points per set.

"She's been amazing for us and she's definitely one of our go-getters," head coach Morgan Hooe said. "Coming in as a junior college transfer is pretty tough to jump in and buy into the culture, but Lee has been that standard and kind of kept us that way."

Ivakina isn't the typical transfer student, as she is originally from Varna, Bulgaria.

With help from a recruiting agency back home in Bulgaria, she began her collegiate career at Navarro College, a junior college in Corsicana, Texas.

"After I was done with JUCO, my coaches helped me contact UAA, and now I'm here," Ivakina said. "I have enjoyed my time here; it's very nice."

Since the part of her home country where she grew up is close to the Black Sea, playing in Texas felt normal, but living in Alaska has been "quite different" and requires her to bundle up a lot more.

"It's getting cold here, so it's quite miserable, but it's been great and I've enjoyed it," Ivakina said.

She takes pride in being a leader on and off the court for the Seawolves as a playmaker and with her words, even though English isn't her first language.

"I love it, and I'm trying to do better every day, honestly," Ivakina said.

Her ability to uplift the team during matches goes beyond just her individual ability to score.

"Even if Lee has an off game, she's always contributing in some other fashion," Hooe said. "Sometimes we have to bring her out of the back row to give her a little break, but for the most part, she's our go-to gal."

Ivakina has recorded double-digit kills in 16 of 24 matches this season. Against Western Washington on Sept. 24, she tied her single-match career-high mark of 25 kills and set a new single-match career-high in points with 27.

"Lee is a phenomenal hitter," senior setter Hannah Trotter said. "She's one of those people who I know I can set her the ball no matter what and she's going to try her hardest to get the kill."

Trotter admires Ivakina's fearlessness and willingness to rise to the occasion when the team needs her the most, whether they're up, down or tied on the scoreboard.

"She's not afraid to hit the ball hard, she's going to make herself an option," Trotter said. "She's one of those hitters that every single ball, she comes to me and says, 'Set me the next ball,' and while I can't give her all of them, when you have someone like that (who) wants it that much for one, themselves and two, the team, I think it's a game changer because she has that competitive nature."

Both Trotter and Ivakina will play their final two home games as Seawolves this week when UAA takes on St. Martin's on Thursday night and Western Oregon on Saturday night.

Although she won't have any family or friends from Bulgaria in attendance when she takes the court in the main gym at the Alaska Airlines Center on Saturday night, Ivakina still expects it to be an emotional experience.

"I'm very, very excited; I'm not ready for senior night," she said. "I love the crowd, and I love playing at home; it's one of the highlights of my stay here."

Trotter also feels bittersweet about her Seawolf career coming to an end, but she's grateful and excited for the team's final two opportunities to prove themselves in front of their home fans.

"It's a fun arena to play in, it's a fun community to be in and it's just a fun environment," she said. "I think when we have things like this, it just kind of shows how special this school and this state are."

The Seawolves will close out the regular season on the road next week and are focused on winning, or at least earning another share of the Great Northwest Athletic Conference title for the second year in a row and qualifying for the Western Regional tournament.

"We're right on the cusp of potentially going to the West Regions, so if we can hopefully do our best for the next several games that we have, maybe we can see ourselves in the West," Hooe said.

#2757. Smyth to return as starting Saints kicker on Sunday

New Orleans Saints head coach Kellen Moore has confirmed Charlie Smyth will start as kicker against the Cleveland Browns on Sunday.

Smyth had been on the Saints' practice squad with Daniel Carlson earning the starting slot for the season, but inconsistent form saw the 31-year-old's contract terminated earlier this week.

On Monday, Moore said he would "evaluate" the situation and at Wednesday's news briefing, he announced Smyth will start against the Browns at the Caesars Superdome this week.

"Charlie will kick this week," he confirmed.

"[There have been] tough decisions. Daniel has made some really big kicks for us - it's just the consistency thing. I just felt this is the best decision for our football team going forward.

"These things are never easy, but Charlie has done this for us at the backend of last year, so he will be ready to go."

Smyth, a former Gaelic footballer from Mayobridge in County Down, made his NFL debut last season but returned to the practice roster after the Saints elected to bring in Carlson for the start of the current campaign after spending five seasons with the Las Vegas Raiders.

Carlson had a success rate of 66% from 12 field goal attempts and his only perfect game with the boot came in September, when he landed three kicks against the Baltimore Ravens.

He made 16 of his 18 extra point attempts for the struggling Saints, who have just two wins from seven games this season, before his contract was terminated on Tuesday.

With the Saints opting not bringing any additional kickers into their squad, Smyth returns to the starting line-up.

The 25-year-old made 12 of 16 field goals and was successful on each of his 13 extra point attempts as a starter last season, after his historic NFL debut last November.

He had a mixed pre-season and dropped back down the practice squad after Carlson was selected to be the Saints' first choice following a kicking competition ahead of the NFL season.

"When guys aren't playing these games, not everyone gets to see all the work they do during the week," Moore said of Smyth's development in the practice squad.

"They still have a plan. Charlie has a plan going into every week [for] how is he going to get better, similar to [quarterback] Tyler Shough when he was on practice squad.

"The guys who don't play on Sunday still have a plan to develop and get better, then opportunities like this present themselves and they are ready."

#2756. Now 26 years old, Tyrese Maxey’s career trajectory remains remarkable

Happy Birthday to Tyrese Maxey, who turns 26 years old today.

Between the Jaylen Brown trade and LeBron James signing for a minimum deal this offseason, it became easy to overlook just how important Maxey is to the Philadelphia 76ers franchise. The first eight games of the regular season have fixed that issue, with Tyrese exploding for 29.1 points per game on 50.3 percent shooting from the field, while also averaging 4.1 rebounds, 5.3 assists, 1.8 steals and 0.9 blocks. Maxey is even shooting well below career numbers from behind the arc over this small sample, so we could very well be looking at him averaging over 30 points per game this season. With the Sixers currently sitting with a 7-1 record, Tyrese is rightfully getting some early MVP conversation buzz. As Jaylen Brown said yesterday, this is Maxey's team.

We often neglect to account for how improbable Maxey reaching this point truly is within the bigger picture. He slipped to 21st overall in the 2020 NBA draft; players taken outside the lottery rarely reach All-NBA levels. Shooting was regarded as a weakness in his game and Tyrese has worked to become one of the absolute best shooters in the world. Maxey is a 6-foot-2 guard who was viewed as a defensive liability, and has now progressed to being a genuinely positively impactful player on that end of the court. The one percent better every day mantra could just be cute slogan for 99.9 percent of the population; for Maxey, he actually goes out and achieves it.

Maxey is already the career three-point field goals leader in Sixer franchise history (936 and counting). He currently sits 12th in franchise history on the scoring list, and barring injury, he'll be at least 10th at the end of the season. At his current trajectory, it would only take about six more seasons of similar play following this one for Tyrese to become the franchise's all-time leading scorer. We could very well be in the middle of watching the most prolific Sixers career ever.

Looking back, it's fair to argue Maxey saved this franchise from bottoming out for at least the next half decade. Without him around, the locker room may very well have imploded during the rough patch of recent years. Maybe the front office decides to do the full reset and strip things down to the studs. Mike Gansey likely doesn't think it's worth giving up future draft capital to acquire Jaylen Brown. LeBron James certainly doesn't sign here without Maxey's presence. It's a dark sliding doors world where Mike Muscala doesn't hit that shot and Tyrese Maxey doesn't end up a Sixer. Happy Birthday, Tyrese! We are very, very glad to have you.

#2751. Billions quietly exchanged in major Mavericks ownership twist

A change of power has commenced in Dallas.

Sivan and Patrick Dumont are now in controlling power of the Mavericks after buying Mirian Adelson's shares in October, giving the family a 69% ownership share, ESPN reported Tuesday.

"It's really about long-term investment in the city of Dallas," Dumont told the outlet. "The Dumont family is really committed to building what we hope is the best arena in the NBA, a really significant investment in the city that will help create the new 40-year home for the Dallas Mavericks, a great experience for our fans and inspire our team to win championships. That's really what we're focused on. We had the opportunity, and we're really excited about it."

Dallas Mavericks owner Miriam Adelson, left, sits with daughter Sivan Ochshorn and governor Patrick Dumont during a game in Macao on Sunday, Oct. 11, 2026. The Dallas Morning News via Gett

Adelson, who had little involvement with the franchise despite her role, bought the majority share from Mark Cuban in December 2023 along with her daughter Sivan and son-in-law Patrick.

Thus, she still remains close to the team.

Cuban has since said he regretted selling after the Dumonts and Adelson pushed him out of the team's basketball and business operations side, but he still maintains a 27% share of the team.

Per the terms of the 2023 sale, Dumont has the option to buy another 20% share from the Shark Tank figure at the original $3.5 billion valuation.

Based on his comments, it's something the team governor plans to do.

"Our plan is to look at that and to do that over time," Dumont said. "I just have to tell you: I love Mark. He's been a great family friend for a really long time. He's just a super passionate Mavs fan and he's frustrated. I respect it. We're going to hopefully do him proud. That's my goal — to make all of our Mavs fans super proud about the success of the team and win a championship."

Dumont has issued various changes among the franchise during his tenure.

The Mavericks are expected to debut a new arena at the Valley View Mall in time for the 2031-32 season after calling American Airlines Center home since 2001. The team's practice facility and business offices are also undergoing renovations.

Dallas Mavericks governor Patrick Dumont watches play during the second half of an NBA game against the Golden State Warriors on Saturday, March 23, 2026. Getty Images

He is also tagged to the sudden trade of Luka Doncic, coming just months after the Mavericks' run to the 2024 NBA Finals. Dumont approved of former general manager Nico Harrison's decision to ship him to the Lakers — a bombshell trade that blew up the league overnight in February 2025.

The governor has since pivoted to put forth efforts in building around reigning Rookie of the Year, Cooper Flagg. After firing Harrison a year ago, former Raptors executive Masai Ujiri has all the power moving forward and has been given permission to make drastic changes.

"This is Masai Ujiri's team," Dumont said. "He's got a ring. He knows what he's doing."

The Mavericks are 4-3 heading into Wednesday's clash with the Spurs and will aim to make the playoffs after finishing just 26-56 last season.

#2749. Can Arch Manning Finally Silence the Doubters?

Arch Manning entered the 2026 college football season with enormous expectations, but the Texas quarterback has experienced plenty of criticism throughout the year. After being viewed as one of the top Heisman Trophy contenders entering the season, Manning has faced questions about his consistency, decision-making and ability to lead Texas to a national championship.

Now, after putting together his best performance of the season, Manning has an opportunity to change the conversation.

The Texas quarterback has had an up-and-down season, including difficult performances in losses to Oklahoma and Florida. Those struggles led to questions about whether head coach Steve Sarkisian should consider making a quarterback change.

However, Sarkisian continued to show confidence in his starting quarterback, and Manning responded with two consecutive victories.

His latest performance against Mississippi State was especially encouraging. Manning completed 16 of 20 passes for 220 yards and three touchdowns without throwing an interception in Texas' 31-3 victory.

It was arguably his most complete performance of the season and showed what the Texas offense can look like when Manning plays confidently.

There is no denying that Manning has faced a different level of pressure because of his last name. Coming from the Manning family, expectations have followed him throughout his college career.

However, talent has never been the biggest question surrounding Manning. His arm strength, athleticism and ability to make plays outside the pocket have always made him one of the most exciting quarterbacks in college football.

The challenge now is putting everything together consistently against the toughest competition.

Texas still has important games remaining, and Manning will have opportunities to prove that his recent improvement is here to stay.

If he continues making smart decisions, protecting the football and creating explosive plays, the doubters could quickly become believers.

Manning has already shown flashes of greatness. Now he needs to show that he can deliver consistently.

The talent is there, but the next few weeks could determine whether Arch Manning finally silences the criticism and becomes the quarterback Texas expected him to be.

#2747. Game Preview: Suns get another shot at the team that started their season spiraling

Who: Phoenix Suns (1-4) vs. Portland Trail Blazers (5-1)

Where: Mortgage Matchup Center — Phoenix, Arizona

Watch: Arizona's Family 3TV, Arizona's Family Sports

The Suns are back at it on Wednesday evening as they return to the site of their only victory thus far this season. Home. What lies before them is a seven-game homestand, an opportunity to stabilize the front end of the season before things spiral into an unrecoverable tailspin.

Their opponent? The very team that sent them down this worrisome path in the first place, the Portland Trail Blazers, who led by as many as 52 points before handing Phoenix a 41-point defeat in the season opener.

In some respects, it feels like we're watching two teams heading in completely different directions. Last season, these teams met in the Play-In Tournament, with the Suns holding home-court advantage as the seventh seed. But the Trail Blazers, led by Deni Avdija, dispatched Phoenix with relative ease, putting the Suns in a position no team wants to find itself in. They were suddenly at risk of becoming the first seventh seed to miss the postseason entirely. Thankfully, Phoenix won its next game, earning a date with the Oklahoma City Thunder. We all know how that went.

But as this season has gotten underway, Portland has continued to build on the momentum it established a season ago, racing out to a 5-1 start. Meanwhile, the Suns find themselves on the opposite end of the spectrum at 1-4.

It's a small sample size, of course. At any point during an NBA season, a team can go 1-4 over a five-game stretch, and while there might be some concern, you probably don't think too much of it. But this is the foundation of the season. This is when we're getting to know what a team looks like, what it's capable of, what it does well, and where its opportunities for improvement lie.

When you look at the Portland Trail Blazers through that same lens, you see a roster constructed in a manner that makes sense, one capable of absorbing an injury or two without completely compromising its identity. That's exactly what they're dealing with. Ja Morant and Shaedon Sharpe are unavailable for this matchup, yet Portland possesses enough quality depth to remain competitive. Their absences don't necessarily derail what they're trying to accomplish.

The Suns, meanwhile, are experiencing something entirely different at the center position. Although Mark Williams is the only player listed as out on the injury report, his absence has created a ripple effect throughout the roster, one that becomes especially concerning when facing a team like Portland.

Perhaps Phoenix can use what happened in the season opener as motivation heading into Wednesday night. Nobody enjoys being embarrassed in the manner the Suns were, especially when the team responsible for that embarrassment is coming into your building. The question becomes: What are you going to do about it?

Mark Williams — OUT (Left Shoulder Surgery)

Jordan Goodwin — QUESTIONABLE (Right Calf Soreness)

Rasheer Fleming — QUESTIONABLE (Left Ankle Sprain)

Shaedon Sharpe — OUT (Right Meniscus Surgery)

The Suns' backcourt continues to be a point of evaluation for both the organization and the fan base. Jalen Green had what appeared to be a solid performance in the Suns' last outing, finishing with 11 points, eight rebounds, eight assists, and four turnovers. His playmaking abilities felt much more refined, although he certainly received some help from his friends, who were knocking down the shots he created for them.

The Suns' backcourt continues to be a point of evaluation for both the organization and the fan base. Jalen Green had what appeared to be a solid performance in the Suns' last outing, finishing with 11 points, eight rebounds, eight assists, and four turnovers. His playmaking abilities felt much more refined, although he certainly received some help from his friends, who were knocking down the shots he created for them.

Green finished the game with 11 potential assists, meaning his teammates failed to convert only three times when he put them in a position to score. That's a 73% conversion rate, which is pretty remarkable. Prior to Monday's game, his assist conversion rate was 48%.

But then you look at the other aspects of Green's game, and you can't help but wonder why he can't seem to put everything together at the same time.  He shot 1-of-6 from the free-throw line and 0-of-4 from beyond the arc, posting a true shooting percentage of 33.1%. Make a couple of those shots, and we're potentially talking about a much different outcome against Golden State.

So once again, I'll be watching how this backcourt performs, particularly whether Green can combine his improved playmaking with the scoring efficiency this team desperately needs.

I'll also be paying close attention to how Phoenix defends Portland, because the last time these teams met, the Suns were utterly atrocious on that end of the floor. You can chalk some of that up to it being the season opener, with players still trying to understand their assignments, responsibilities, and positioning within the defensive scheme. But regardless of the circumstances, Phoenix surrendered far too many wide-open three-pointers.

And Damian Lillard was more than happy to oblige, connecting on 6-of-10 attempts from beyond the arc.

Trail Blazers Shot Chart vs. Suns: 10/21/26 | hoopsjunkie.io

It's simple. This one comes down to rebounding. The Phoenix Suns are the worst rebounding team in the league by percentage, and they're going up against the best in the Portland Trail Blazers.

Phoenix has struggled mightily on the glass, both offensively and defensively. This roster doesn't have many players who can go up and simply snatch the shit out of the basketball. Instead, seemingly every rebound becomes an adventure in which poor positioning forces someone to tip the ball toward a teammate or desperately try to tap it back to themselves.

Meanwhile, Portland has long, rangy defenders who understand how to position themselves and secure rebounds, creating second-chance opportunities that can bury an opponent. It's something the Trail Blazers did well last season, and they're picking up right where they left off.

If the Suns want any chance of winning this game, they're going to have to earn every rebound through focus, positioning, and our good old friend, "give a shit".

There's nothing I've seen thus far this season that gives me confidence the Suns are going to win this game. They've been atrocious at defending the three-point line, their rebounding has been equally concerning, and those are two areas in which the Portland Trail Blazers have excelled early this season.

Perhaps this will be like last year, when everything came together in the fifth game. The defensive intensity started to manifest itself in a way that was both replicable and exciting to watch, giving us a glimpse of what this team could become.

I'd love to believe we're on the verge of witnessing something similar. I simply don't see it.

#2741. A missed sack, a trash-talk penalty and a learning lesson for the young Miami Dolphins

MIAMI GARDENS, Fla. (AP) — Chris Johnson has watched the play over and over.

The Miami Dolphins rookie cornerback had what seemed to be an easy sack of Patriots quarterback Drake Maye on a corner blitz last Sunday.

Johnson had nothing but open field as he ran toward Maye, who was about to throw the ball on fourth-and-2. Johnson dove at Maye's feet but somehow missed the quarterback altogether, resulting in a Patriots completion in what was eventually a 24-19 comeback win for New England.

"I don't enjoy seeing it," Johnson, a first-round pick, said on Wednesday. "It's an embarrassing play, but at the end of the day, I feel like it's a reminder to me what happens when you mess up in a big moment. So I can't wait to get on the field and make it up."

Johnson is a versatile corner who has played mostly at nickel but also lines up on the boundary at times. He called the missed sack an "out-of-character" play, and Dolphins coach Jeff Hafley said it was a "one-time" mistake that coaches can correct.

The play certainly wasn't the reason that Miami blew a 10-point halftime lead en route to its sixth loss of the season. Hafley referred to a number of missed opportunities that kept the Dolphins from holding onto their lead Sunday, which he hopes his young players can learn from.

"You continue to learn from stuff like that in those moments," quarterback Malik Willis said Wednesday, "and what you feel in those moments and where you need to dig deep and continue to strive for it. (That's) why we need to execute every time we get a chance and why every series matters and why every play comes down — it matters. And a lot of times it gets blamed on one or two plays, that's not the reality of it. It takes the entire composure of a game to really, dedicate whether you win or lose. So I think we just gotta continue to push forward. That's it."

Another teaching moment came when second-year running back Ollie Gordon was hit with an unsportsmanlike conduct penalty for trash talking to a Patriots defender following a 5-yard carry in the third quarter.

The play turned a third-and-5 into third-and-20, which the Dolphins didn't convert while clinging to a three-point lead. Hafley removed Gordon from the game for a play, and the running back appeared visibly upset while Willis spoke to him on the sideline.

Hafley said he spoke with Gordon several times since then, noting he and coaches will teach the running back how to better manage his emotions.

"It was one of those things where I took it too far," Gordon said Wednesday. "Sometimes you've got to be quiet and go back to the huddle, and that's one of the things that I've got to get better at."

Rookie left guard Kadyn Proctor returned to practice Wednesday after being sidelined in Week 8 with a concussion.

Hafley said Proctor, drafted 12th overall in April, is advancing through the concussion protocol, and he was listed as a full practice participant on Wednesday's injury report.

His return could be a boost for the Dolphins run game, which had topped 100 yards rushing in five straight games before being limited to 74 yards against New England.

Mitchell Robinson (4) of the Boston Celtics slam dunks against the Houston Rockets during a game in Houston, Monday, Nov. 2, 2026. Getty Images

Joe Mazzulla of the Boston Celtics high-fives Mitchell Robinson (4) during the first half against the Chicago Bulls on Friday, Oct. 30, 2026, in Boston. Getty Images

The Celtics have a net rating of minus-18.8 when he's on the court compared to compared to plus-19.4 when he's off the court, per screwball.com.

The Knicks were better with Robinson on the court in each of the last two seasons per that metric, and his minus-38.2 net is the worst of his career.

"I would say for his offensive rebounding impact, we have him a little bit further away from the rim, setting screens and playmaking for us on the perimeter as part of our offense," Mazzulla said recently, per the Globe. "I think that has a little bit to do with it.

"But as far as the two-man game, it's just something to work on."

The Celtics have managed a 5-2 start despite Robinson's issues with their new system, and have a soft stretch coming up with three games against Milwaukee (twice) and Charlotte before a storyline-filled clash with ex-star Jaylen Brown and the 76ers next Tuesday.

#2732. Heartbreaking defeat! Lizelle Lee's rare wicketkeeping error denies Hobart Hurricanes a Super Over against Melbourne Renegade - Watch

Melbourne Renegades pulled off a dramatic four-wicket victory over Hobart Hurricanes in the Women's Big Bash League (WBBL) on Wednesday, with a rare wicketkeeping infringement on the final delivery denying the hosts a chance to force a Super Over. Just when the Hurricanes appeared to have rescued a thrilling contest, Lizelle Lee's positioning behind the stumps turned the game on its head, handing the Renegades a victory through a no-ball decision at Bellerive Oval.Chasing 155, the Renegades needed one run from the final delivery with scores level at 154. Deandra Dottin failed to connect with Nicola Carey's delivery, and Lee collected the ball behind the stumps. Both teams appeared to be preparing for a Super Over, but the third umpire intervened after television replays showed that Lee had collected the ball in front of the stumps.Under Law 27.3, a wicketkeeper must remain wholly behind the striker's wicket until the ball touches the batter or their bat, passes the wicket, or the batter attempts a run. Any breach of this provision results in a no-ball being called. The decision awarded the Renegades the additional run they needed to seal a four-wicket win without a Super Over.

The dramatic finish followed another crucial twist in the final over. The Hurricanes were penalised for failing to complete their 20 overs within the stipulated time and were allowed only two fielders outside the 30-yard circle for the final three deliveries.After a dot ball, Dottin edged the penultimate delivery towards the deep third boundary. With the boundary rider unavailable because of the fielding restriction, the ball raced away for four, bringing the scores level at 154.Dottin then missed the final delivery, and Lee's infringement completed a remarkable turnaround for the visitors. The Hurricanes were left stunned as the Renegades celebrated their first victory of the WBBL|12 campaign.

Earlier, Hobart Hurricanes posted 154/7 in their 20 overs, with captain Nicola Carey leading the charge in her 150th WBBL appearance. Carey struck 62 off 43 deliveries, providing stability to an innings that saw the Hurricanes lose wickets at regular intervals.West Indian all-rounder Chinelle Henry added a quickfire 36 off just 18 balls to give the hosts a late boost. However, Henry later hobbled off after bowling just one over, which cost 14 runs, with a suspected hip injury.Renegades captain Sophie Molineux led the bowling attack with figures of 4/22, taking her career WBBL tally to 101 wickets. She became only the second player in the franchise's history to reach the 100-wicket milestone, after Molly Strano.

In reply, Hayley Matthews kept the Renegades on course with an impressive 59 off 44 deliveries, while Courtney Webb contributed 29 off 25 balls. The pair helped the visitors build a strong platform before the Hurricanes fought back.The Renegades suffered a dramatic collapse, losing five wickets for 37 runs after reaching 101, bringing the contest back into balance. Matthews was dismissed by Heather Graham, while Molly Strano produced a superb catch-and-bowl effort to remove Molineux.With 33 runs required from the final four overs, the Renegades used the Power Surge to keep the chase alive. Despite the late setbacks, they eventually crossed the line in one of the most unusual finishes of the tournament.The victory gave the Renegades their first win of the WBBL season 12, while the Hurricanes remained without a win after three matches and at the bottom of the standings.

#2728. Next step? Fischer says Nets hope for big return for vets

The Nets rebuild can probably be traced back to June 25, 2024 when Brooklyn made two trades within minutes of each other that signals half steps were over. One sent Mikal Bridges to New York for five first rounders, a first round swap and a second rounder; the other an exchange of firsts with the Rockets that brought back a first rounder and a first round swap they had originally sent Houston in the first James Harden trade.

The second step was the two-year-long tank that helped the Nets get two high — although not as high as they had hoped — firsts in Egor Demin and Mikel Brown Jr. as well as five other late firsts. The hope, at least among fans, was that the losing or at least the deliberate losing would be over. Not yet, apparently, as Brooklyn is off to a 1-6 start and the two rookies have shared the court for only a few minutes. For the second straight year, the Nets are also the youngest team in the league, not a recipe for contention.

Now, into the fan caldron of disappointment, Jake Fischer seemingly dropped some knowledge that would seemingly ensure a longer rebuild. Speaking to Yahoo! Sports, the writer suggested that the next step could be trying to move veterans Michael Porter Jr and/or Julius Randle presumably at the February trade deadline, presumably to a contender, for more assets, presumably more picks or maybe other assets. Scoring, already an issue, and wins would be sacrificed.

"Brooklyn, I would still categorize as a team that is in more of a sell situation. I believe that Brooklyn will hope that a Julius Randle, a Michael Porter Jr. could still potentially draw some real returns," Fischer said.

"The Nets, they don't have their draft pick this year and they're looking to be better and compete in the Eastern Conference, take some strides forward in the East.

"I don't think they're going to be a full sell job right now, but I do think that they are still in a position to solicit trade offers for their veterans to get draft capital, potentially young pieces back in return."

Reading carefully, rather than scanning aggregators' headlines, reveals that Fischer isn't making predictions that the Nets will push hard to move both. He talks about "a Julius Randle," "a Michael Porter Jr." not both. Nor does he specifically mention the trade deadline. Nor does he gauge interest levels on the other side.

Adding to the Nets' cache of assets, of course, does make sense from a number of aspects. If, as Sean Marks has said, the ultimate goal is a player or players who will "automatically and absolutely change the trajectory of the franchise." then the accumulation of assets makes sense, particularly in an era where trades, not free agency, is the route to acquiring stars and superstars. Look at all the stars who moved this summer. Only LeBron James signed as a free agent and that of course was an oddity.

Either of the two players would seem to be the ideal final piece for teams hoping to make a final run at a title. Randle and MPJ can put up big numbers and have. Porter Jr. also has a ring. Yeah sure, both have baggage, but their ability to get buckets is undisputed. As Basket News noted,

Porter Jr. is averaging 20.7 points, 7.0 rebounds, 1.4 assists and 1.6 steals, shooting 38.1% from the three-point line.

In the meantime, Randle holds the averages of 20.9 points, 7.7 rebounds and 4.9 assists.

Their contracts also militate for moves unless the Nets see them as part of their future. MPJ will be an unrestricted free agent come July. If the Nets don't plan on extending him — and there's no indication at this point that they do, then trading him for assets in the interim would be imperative. Similarly, Randle has a player option at the end of this season and he too can leave in July if Sean Marks & co. decide not to make him an offer or an offer he believes fair. (MPJ earns $40.8 million, Randle $33.3 million with an option of $35.8 million.)

Of the two, Porter Jr. would seem to be the more likely to return assets. He's nearly four years younger than Randle, although Randle is closer to iron man status, having averaged 68 games a year over the last eight. He also has proven that he can be a leader during his time at Flatbush and Atlantic.

It should also be noted that any trade is unlikely to produce the automatic and absolute game-changer Marks has spoken about. More likely, it'd be the big trade before the bigger trade which might happen in the summer of 2027.

One of the most intriguing but also less than concrete suggestions in Fischer's comment is that the Nets might move away from accumulating draft picks to seeking "potentially young pieces" — that is, undervalued players on rookie deals. After all, they do have 13 firsts and 19 seconds over the next seven years. That strategy has been a key element in a number of recent rebuilds, most notably OKC's acquisition of a young Shai Gilgeous-Alexander, in the trade of Paul George to the Clippers back in June 2019. That one trade got Sam Presti one building block after another from SGA to Aday Mara. We should be so lucky.

Of course the trade deadline — 3:00 p.m. ET on February 11 — is three months away and there are few trades in the first couple of months of any season. The unofficial start of trade season is December 15 for two reasons. It's roughly 20 games into the season when teams understand what their needs are going forward as well as the first day that players signed or resigned during the off-season can be dealt. That expands the pool of players who are available by dozens. That's especially relevant putting together complicated, multi-team deals. Perhaps the spate of early season injuries will move things up, but it's unlikely.

#2726. INTERVIEW: Bayern sales chief Kasper on maintaining Kane hype and pushing other clubs to grow Bundesliga appeal

MÜNCHEN (Bulinews): Bayern Munich's social media pages boomed after Harry Kane won the Ballon d'Or, but now the aim is to retain that level of interest. Tasked with fulfilling that role is the club's Marketing and Sales Officer, Rouven Kasper.

“It's difficult, you always have to look at what’s next. We have had strong momentum and are growing based on our media and commercial departments' clear strategy," Kasper told Bulinews.com at a media round in München.

“We have some disadvantages when it comes to international business, but we just need to work as a team with the Bundesliga and the other clubs. For sure, we have it in our hands to make it as good as possible.

“From recent years, it [development] has been very good. We can see now that we can make strong effects in the international field. This gives us even more power so we can invest into different platforms and new products.

“Maybe we can even open infrastructure in other continents, because when you have an office and people there you can learn more about the [local] market. Then you can adapt better. This is our next step," he pointed out.

The Bayern chief was also quick to demand greater output from Bundesliga clubs outside of Bayern and Borussia Dortmund. Kasper senses the other 16 teams in the topflight could play an important role in growing the league's appeal.

“The league needs to be better and bring more reach. We [Bayern Munich] are the spearhead of German football. Together with other the other clubs and the league, we need to be more active in the market.

“When you compare the last two years, there were 13 Premier League clubs playing abroad outside the UK [in pre-season] while Bundesliga clubs had five. At the end, it’s about people and being a community.

“We want to be approachable. We want to talk to the people and be there as the Bundesliga. Bayern along with Dortmund can’t do that alone, so we need more activity from the other clubs in different markets.

“The UK teams, also the Spanish [La Liga] are doing. We are doing our part, not only in the summer but also the winter. It requires team spirit and I think it needs to be pushed a little bit more,” said Kasper, claiming a need for cooperation.

#2725. The hits keep coming for wild-card hopefuls chasing the Packers

Heading into Week 9, the Green Bay Packers—and the rest of the NFC North—would make the NFL playoffs if the season ended now. Per the league's website, the NFL projects the Packers make the playoffs about half the time in simulations, with the Minnesota Vikings (81 percent), Chicago Bears (82 percent), and Detroit Lions (80 percent) well ahead.

Here's the good news: The Packers have a full-game lead over the next two teams breathing down their neck for the final wild-card spot, the Seattle Seahawks and Los Angeles Rams. On paper, it's a tight race between these teams, as the Seahawks are given a 47 percent chance of making the postseason, and the Rams are at 46 percent.

With how the landscape is playing out right now, the three wildcard spots in the NFC will likely all come down to North or West teams, and the South and East will just get one playoff team apiece. That means that two of Minnesota, Chicago, Detroit, Green Bay, Seattle or Los Angeles will be on the outside looking in at the end of the year.

Here's another bit of fortunate (I'll hesitate to call it good) news for the Packers, though: The Seahawks and Rams are banged up.

Already, it's been announced that Seattle quarterback Sam Darnold will miss next week's game against the Arizona Cardinals with a quad injury and is considered week-to-week. Darnold's backup, Drew Lock, is also dealing with an elbow injury. Per the NFL's projections, the Seahawks' chances of making the playoffs drop from 47 percent to 29 percent if they lose this weekend.

On the Rams' end of things, cornerback Jaylen Watson, their big $51 million free agent signing, will have shoulder surgery, as announced by head coach Sean McVay on Wednesday, and will not return this season. If, for whatever reason, they lose to the Washington Commanders, the Rams' playoff chances will drop from 46 percent to 31 percent.

Packers fans might not like it, but the team is firmly in the "already caring about the results of other teams' games" portion of the season — and honestly has been for a couple of weeks now. The Seahawks and Rams are the only legit threats to push Green Bay out of the postseason, at least for now, and there hasn't been a lot of positive news to come out about those clubs early on in Week 9.

#2719. Robert Griffin III Gets Painfully Honest About Baltimore Ravens’ Biggest Problem

Oct 30, 2025; Miami Gardens, Florida, USA; Baltimore Ravens quarterback Lamar Jackson (8) warms up before a game against the Miami Dolphins at Hard Rock Stadium. Mandatory Credit: Nathan Ray Seebeck-Imagn Images

That forced undrafted rookie Joe Fagnano into his NFL debut, where he completed 10 of 21 passes for 91 yards and threw an interception. Baltimore finished with only 144 total yards as Buffalo pulled away after halftime.

Jan 7, 2023; Jacksonville, Florida, USA; Monday Night Countdown analyst Robert Griffin III wears a Buffalo Bills safety Damar Hamlin (3) jersey for a broadcast before a game featuring the Tennessee Titans and Jacksonville Jaguars at TIAA Bank Field. Mandatory Credit: Nathan Ray Seebeck-USA TODAY Sports

Griffin shared his thoughts about how injuries have become Baltimore's biggest problem this 2026 NFL season. This can be seen in a post he made on X.

"Only thing holding back the Baltimore Ravens is injuries," Griffin wrote. "Prayers up for Lamar Jackson

The Ravens signed Austin Reed to the practice squad while assessing their options ahead of a short turnaround. Baltimore is scheduled to face the Jacksonville Jaguars on Thursday night, leaving little time to settle its quarterback plan. Huntley's availability remains uncertain, while Fagnano is working through an injury of his own after taking over against Buffalo.

Griffin's remarks underscore the challenge facing a team built around an explosive quarterback whose availability has repeatedly shaped its season. The Ravens must now navigate a difficult stretch without Jackson and determine whether their remaining options can keep the season alive. With a demanding schedule ahead, Baltimore's response to the latest injury crisis could define its campaign, especially as the trade deadline approaches and quarterback reinforcements remain a possibility, potentially soon.

#2718. For today and tomorrow, Labaron Philion needs more minutes

The Sixers are 7-1 after Monday night's 126-107 victory against Chicago. Three of their next four games are against fellow Eastern Conference contenders in Detroit, Cleveland and Boston. Perhaps for that reason, Nick Nurse might not make drastic changes to a rotation that keeps winning. After all, the Sixers haven't opened a playoff round at home since 2023 so locking up wins against other teams in the Eastern Conference now can only help for seeding later. However, there's one glaring mistake with Nurse's rotation that he needs to address.

When it comes to the bench, Nurse is playing Anfernee Simons, Dean Wade and Kentavious Caldwell-Pope regularly. These are veterans who figure to factor into the playoff rotation as well come the spring. However, whether Labaron Philon sees the court in April and beyond or not, the rookie out of Alabama should be seeing more than the eight minutes per game he's currently averaging. Even in a 19-point win against the Bulls, Philon played just two minutes.

While Philon was drafted towards the end of the first round this past summer, now's the time to find out what kind of player he could become. Of course, he can always grow into a bigger role next season and beyond, but should the Sixers set him on a path that's essentially burning a year of his rookie contract? Maybe that's a harsh way of phrasing things after only eight games, but it's the realest way of saying Philon isn't playing nearly enough.

For context, Tyrese Maxey was drafted 21st overall in 2020 which is just one spot earlier than Philon's 22nd overall in 2026. In the 2020-21 season, Maxey's rookie year, Maxey played 15 minutes per game and started eight games. That's a considerable uptick from the amount of minutes Philon is currently playing. Additionally, under Nick Nurse in 2024-25, Jared McCain played 25.7 minutes per game as a rookie in an injury-shortened season that saw him still start eight games. Neither of these comparisons is perfect as McCain was nearly a lottery pick having been selected 15th overall in 2024 and Maxey would have probably been a lottery pick under non-COVID circumstances in his draft eligible season in college.

Part of the reason we're saying the Sixers have a backup center problem yet again is because they whiffed on the Johni Broome pick recently and Adem Bona doesn't seem to have any offensive skillset. Granted, those two players were recent second-rounders by Philadelphia, but the point stands. You want to get your draft picks on the court in their rookie seasons in the regular season when your rotation should be deeper. It's cheap production if they're good right away, gives you more clarity on what they could develop into down the line, and would also make it easier to move off of them in trades while they still have some trade value if the early returns aren't to your liking.

In the short term, don't we all agree Maxey could use more of a breather than what he's currently getting? Maxey is averaging a whopping 37.4 minutes per game which is currently second in the NBA to only Denver's Jamal Murray. Maxey's injury history is nowhere near as lengthy as Joel Embiid's, but that doesn't mean Maxey wouldn't benefit from seeing his minutes scaled back a bit. Maxey's also doesn't seem like the kind of player that would be concerned about various individual accolades that might not come his way if his playing time decreased. More importantly, everyone can agree Maxey needs to be healthy and full go come playoff time for Philadelphia to make a deep run.

In the long term, wouldn't it be great if Philon developed into a sixth man at the very least for this team? Remember how awful Philly's bench was in the playoffs last year? Philon represents a long-term solution to what's been a long-term problem for the Sixers even if it's hard to see him ever getting on the court with long-term building blocks in Maxey, VJ Edgecombe and Jaylen Brown all at the same time.

Make no mistake, winning is fun and the Sixers are doing a lot of it right now. But the best teams in every sport can fuse the present with the future and win in the current season and in seasons of tomorrow. I get it Sixers fans, the current season is probably all that's on your mind and I can't blame you as the Sixers have as good a chance as any other team does to win the NBA championship in 2026-27. But there's going to be basketball played in 2027 and beyond and Labaron Philon was drafted to be a part of that basketball for Philly so this season should also be about finding out what role Philon can have within this organization's long-term plans.

#2717. Former Steelers RB Merril Hoge joins in praise of Mike McCarthy

The Pittsburgh Steelers are 6-2 and in prime position to win the AFC North in back-to-back seasons.

Aaron Rodgers has turned back the clock and is playing at a solid level, leading the Steelers offense to four consecutive victories. People didn't know what to expect with Mike Tomlin out of the picture and Pittsburgh's own Mike McCarthy taking over. More of the same? A better-executed offense? The first losing season in over two decades? So far, so good.

Apologies and credit where credit is due have been the main theme of the bye week for many in the media who were wrong, at least to this point, about the 6-2 team. NFL analyst and former offensive guard Damien Woody is surprised by how McCarthy has anchored the veteran team.

"I have to admit, I did not envision the Pittsburgh Steelers playing as well as they're playing so far as we approach the halfway point," Woody said on The OG Football Show. "I got to give my flowers to Mike McCarthy."

The Steelers offense ranks 13th in total yards per game (351.8) and 14th in rushing (117.9). After years of failing to run the football, McCarthy and offensive line coach James Campen are delivering a legitimate running game.

"Mike McCarthy has done a hell of a job," Woody said. "People frown upon him, but all Mike McCarthy has done is win in Green Bay; he won in Dallas, and now in his first year in Pittsburgh he has them sitting atop the AFC North… Mike McCarthy, Aaron Rodgers and company are pushing all the right buttons. The Pittsburgh Steelers, to me, are the absolute surprise so far."

Former Steeler and current broadcaster Merril Hoge believes the Super Bowl champion coach belongs in Canton for his decades of success in three cities.

"If you took his resume and his credentials and stacked it up to Hall of Famers, it's very similar and better in some ways," Hoge said. "He gets no credit for that. Nobody thinks of him like that. But he can stack up against not only great ones that are still coaching today, but those that sit in the Hall."

The Steelers lead the NFL in sacks. Jaylen Warren is in contention for Offensive Player of the Year and holds more rushing yards than Derrick Henry, Jonathan Taylor, and Christian McCaffrey.

Pittsburgh's schedule will sharpen with games against Philadelphia, Denver, Houston, Jacksonville, and Baltimore (twice) coming up. The Steelers always have difficult battles against the Ravens and haven't won in Philadelphia since 1965.

The Steelers are exceeding expectations, and historically, 90% of teams that start 6-2 make the playoffs. Are the pieces coming together to make a playoff run for the first time in a full decade?

#2714. Myles Garrett, Terrance Ferguson to practice Wednesday, while Rams CB Jaylen Watson will have season-ending surgery

Los Angeles Rams head coach Sean McVay provided injury updates for his team on Wednesday.

The updates were highlighted by both defensive end Myles Garrett and tight end Terrance Ferguson practicing in a limited capacity. McVay also told reporters that cornerback Jaylen Watson will have season-ending shoulder surgery.

Garrett has been sidelined since Week 2 with a knee injury that required arthroscopic surgery and landed him on injured reserve. The defensive end had to sit out a minimum of four games on IR, but he has missed the past seven games.

The injury's timing hurt even more for fans excited to see Garrett paired with the recently unretired Aaron Donald. As fate would have it, Donald wasn't ready to make his season debut until Week 2, so after Garrett was added to IR, Donald returned from 2.5 years of retirement.

Garrett and Donald could finally debut together this week against the Washington Commanders, depending on how Garrett's first week back at practice goes.

Ferguson has also been on injured reserve because of an ankle injury. He hasn't played since Week 3 and is one of several offensive skill-position injuries Los Angeles has been dealing with.

The Rams have a deep tight end room, but Ferguson caught seven passes for 63 yards and a touchdown before getting hurt.

While McVay announced two players are close to returning to game action, he also said the Rams will be without one player for the remainder of the season.

Watson will have season-ending shoulder surgery after getting multiple medical opinions. The cornerback has played in seven games this season and has 24 total tackles. He is one of only four Los Angeles players with an interception this season, logging his lone pick against the Buffalo Bills.

Los Angeles heads into this weekend's road game at Washington with a 4-4 record. The Rams entered the season as this year's Super Bowl favorite, but currently sit third in the NFC West.
