#2685. Beware of the Lion – TSV 1860 Munich can still mix with the Bundesliga's best

MÜNCHEN (Bulinews): On our excursion to München last weekend, naturally topping the list of priorities was the Bundesliga's famed Der Klassiker as FC Bayern Munich hosted Borussia Dortmund on Saturday, 31 October. One could be forgiven for having tunnel vision.

To that, I would say do that at your own peril. The Friday prior to the bright lights of the Allianz Arena also served up a footballing delight in the vibrant southeastern suburb of Giesing. The might of TSV 1860 Munich taking on Regionalliga Bavaria rivals TSV Buchbach.

Four tier of German football you say? Fear not. A whopping 15,000 fans – of which maybe 1000 are Buchbach supporters – filter into the Grünwalder Stadion. The noise is already reaching fever pitch as we wind our way up the concrete steps of the South Stand.

Shortly after, the whistle blows to get the ball rolling. The football is nothing short of brilliant either. That blend of gritty 50-50 duels with no shirking out, coupled with some technical excellence. Namely, Buchbach's No.10 Tobias Heiland who neatly curled in the opening goal.

𝘋𝘪𝘦 𝘓ö𝘸𝘦𝘯 (The Lions) hit back in a flash. Ex-FC Augsburg star Florian Niederlechner and Felix Weber find the net. Still, Buchbach find a response. Heiland again with a sweet half-volley on the edge of the box before Samed Bahar's drive finds the bottom corner.

In need of a refreshment after a half that ended 3-2 to the guests, we make our way to the kiosk. As if the night couldn't get any more interesting, my colleague Manuel Veth of The Gegenpressing Podcast and I happen to bump into the referee's father there.

Overhearing some 1860 fans mulling over his son's first-half calls, he smiles but you sense a hint of paternal worry. The arbiter in question is 26-year-old Johannes Wagner – it was his first time officiating at this level. Might I add, he went on to do an exemplary job of it too.

Into the catacombs we go for an interval meeting with 1860 president Gernot Mang. There's no fancy murals or glitzy design to the interior. You must duck under the stand's support beams to avoid hitting your head. But that's football. Real football. I find it charming.

Mang is a charismatic, calmly spoken but passionate man who confesses his and the club's aim of returning to the Bundesliga – something they've missed since 2004. This isn't some crazed pipe dream. It's a mission. It may take time, but Giesing wasn't built in a day.

Back to our press box for the second helping of what seemed destined to be inevitable chaos. That's exactly what happens. Both sides had chances but Arian Calderon heads in from six yards for the hosts. We're tired at 3-3 with 8 minutes to play plus injury time.

With the clock hitting 90+4 minutes, space was afforded to Lance Fiedler. Buchbach stood off enabling the left-back to hammer a shot inside the left upright. Cue pandemonium in the terraces. You can't help but get caught up in it. Even the steward beside us delights.

Chants of "𝘌𝘪𝘯𝘮𝘢𝘭 𝘓ö𝘸𝘦, 𝘐𝘮𝘮𝘦𝘳 𝘓ö𝘸𝘦" (Once a Lion, forever a Lion) fill the cool Munich air. The three points sees them rise to second in the table. After the emotional rollercoaster, the party continues in Bamboleo – a 𝘒𝘯𝘦𝘪𝘱𝘦 – your unpretentious, cozy local matchday bar.

Sure, the allure of FC Bayern and BVB may have dominated the weekend. But in a way, the 1860 atmosphere is unfiltered and unmatched. One could argue it even surpasses their arch rivals in red and merits a hasty return to Germany's topflight.

Things may not be entirely harmonious behind the scenes after majority shareholder Hasan Ismaik's exit. However, the football drops jaws and the fans remain unwavering. A lion is the king of the jungle. It's fearless, resilient and untamable – TSV 1860 Munich embodies it all.

#2684. Major Setback For LIV 2.0 As BC Partners Pulls Out Of Financing Deal

BC Partners, the investment firm that stepped in to replace the Saudi Public Investment Fund (PIF) as financiers for LIV Golf, have pulled out of their deal with LIV Golf League. BC Partners had originally committed to investing $300 million in LIV Golf League, providing much needed funding for LIV Golf 2.0, the supposed next chapter of LIV Golf League. The move by BC Partners will be a major setback for the continued survival of LIV Golf.

Only a handful of players had publicly committed to playing LIV Golf 2.0. Those players were Richard Bland, Cameron Tringale, Brendan Steele, and Ben Campbell. Many of LIV Golf's stars have since departed the league, including Tyrrell Hatton, Jon Rahm, and Sergio Garcia, with still others having departed even earlier. Those players include Brooks Koepka, Patrick Reed, and Eugenio Chacarra. Still, others remain in limbo, including Dustin Johnson and others who are hoping to negotiate with the Saudi PIF regarding unpaid contracts.

Koepka rejoined the PGA Tour in 2026 under the PGA Tour's returning member program, which included program eligibility requirements such as having won certain tournaments within 2022-2025 as well as a short application window. Many other LIV golfers did not take that route. It's presumed that those ex-LIV golfers with status on the DP World Tour will play on that tour for the 2027 season.

LIV Golf had previously filed for Chapter 11 bankruptcy on September 8, 2026 in order to restructure the league's debts and obligations. The bankruptcy proceedings were originally intended to give LIV Golf time to address prior financial obligations and to complete it's transaction with BC Partners.

A hearing is scheduled in US Bankruptcy Court for LIV Golf on November 5. However, it's likely that without financing from BC Partners, LIV Golf will be headed towards a liquidation of its assets.

#2683. Reviewing the Bears’ PFN grades from Week 8 vs. Seahawks

The Bears made an impact in primetime on Monday night, decisively beating the Seahawks 34-17 to improve their record to 6-2 for the 2026-27 NFL season. It was a convincing win which saw Chicago excel on both sides of the ball, limiting the Seahawks' offense and gashing apart what is generally seen as one of the best defenses in the NFL. There were a lot of positive takeaways out of the All-22, and the analytics reflect that.

Each week, I'll review how Chicago's eligible performers graded by Pro Football Network's Impact Scoring model. Now that the rest of the NFL has caught up, and the numbers have all been crunched, here's how Week 8 looked.

Caleb Williams was an above-average performer at quarterback across the board on Monday night, which is made more impressive considering the quality of the Seahawks' defense. He graded in at least the top 12 in such categories as EPA per dropback, success rate, adjusted net yards per attempt, completion percentage, completion percentage over expected, and passing yards.

It helped that he had three efficient weapons to throw to; though Luther Burden III led all Bears weapons in PFN Impact grading, it was Rome Odunze who had the highest EPA per target, ranking fourth in the metric at +1.07. Likewise, Chicago had two top-ten performances at guard for the week out of both Jonah Jackson and Joe Thuney.

Jeffery Simmons made his Bears debut on Monday, and he absolutely shined on the big stage. His six tackles tied for the most on the team and tied for third among 92 qualified defensive tackles, and he also added a sack into the mix, despite facing a heavy double-team focus from Seattle's offensive line. The two biggest benefactors from Simmons' presence in Week 8 were Montez Sweat and Devin Bush, as both ended up with sacks, and the latter tied Simmons for the Bears' lead in tackles for the night.

The Seahawks picked on Tyrique Stevenson often, as he was targeted ten times and gave up a targeted passer rating of 124.2 for the game. However, strong auxiliary performances from Cam Lewis, Austin Booker, James Lynch, and D'Marco Jackson gave Chicago an all-around solid defensive outing.

Cairo Santos hit all of his field goal and extra point attempts, nailing his two field goal tries from 44 and 38 yards, respectively. His +0.4 field goals made over expected tied for the fourth best in the NFL in Week 8. #2681. 2026-27 NHL season predictions: Expert picks for Stanley Cup playoffs, champion and major awards this season

If not listed, please contact your TV provider.

Following a brief offseason and an expedited preseason, the 2026-27 NHL season has arrived. Before the puck drops on opening night, we have some predictions to make.

If this season feel like it's staring earlier than usual, that's because it is. The NHL is adopting an 84-game regular season, which just means more hockey for the rest of us. That also means a long and arduous journey to the Stanley Cup is ever so slightly more difficult.

So, what will that journey look like? The Carolina Hurricanes are looking to defend their title as Cup champions, but they'll have to contend with a deep Eastern Conference and some real juggernauts in the Western Conference. With that in mind, we logged our expert predictions for the playoff field in each conference -- and there were a few surprises -- before making our Stanley Cup Final selections.

We also called our shot on the winners of each major individual award. Connor McDavid is looking for his fourth Hart Trophy, but he's got some competition from an up-and-coming superstar in his own division. Races for the Norris Trophy and Vezina Trophy should be pretty wide open, and unexpected candidates always emerge throughout the season.

It should be a fun season in the NHL, so follow along with all our coverage at CBS Sports. Let's get things kicked off with the season preview and predictions, which will of course be 100% accurate.

Baumgartner: I don't see many new faces entering the conversation when it comes to the Eastern Conference playoff race. A healthy Florida Panthers side will slot themselves right back towards the top of the Atlantic, and a revamped Washington Capitals side should be able to make some noise in the Metropolitan. Besides that, I have six 2026 playoff teams returning to the dance in 2027.

Nivison: As much as I hate to go with chalk in preseason predictions -- because it rarely happens that way -- there's no denying the top dogs in the West. The Avalanche and Stars are the class of the Central Division, and the Golden Knights should pound a pitiful Pacific Division into a pulp. Things get more interesting behind those clear Cup contenders, and I'm projecting another significant leap forward from the Mammoth. I also think Macklin Celebrini will be successful in dragging the Sharks to the playoffs in his third NHL season.

Nivison -- Columbus Blue Jackets: The Blue Jackets have been agonizingly close to the playoffs in each of the last two seasons, and I think they get over the hump in 2026-27. There are still questions in Columbus. Can Adam Fantilli be a franchise player at center? Is the forward group as a whole good enough to keep pace in a competitive Eastern Conference? Still, I am sure the Blue Jackets have a really strong blue line, led by Norris Trophy winner Zach Werenski, and goaltender Jet Greaves is on track to become a Vezina Trophy candidate. Internal improvements from the young core will then get Columbus above the cut line.

Baumgartner -- San Jose Sharks: The Sharks narrowly missed out on the 2026 Stanley Cup Playoffs, finishing just four points short of the second wild card. San Jose added a good group of veterans over the summer, but the most important offseason move was selecting Ivar Stenberg with the No. 2 pick in the Draft. Stenberg joins the youth movement in San Jose, which is spearheaded by new captain Macklin Celebrini, who broke the Sharks single-season points record with 115 last season. The Sharks are a better team than they were last year, and with Celebrini entering his third season as a legit Hart Trophy contender, this team won't be fun to play against.

Nivison: Yes, the Mammoth have a brutal path coming out of the Central Divison, but just close your eyes and imagine this scenario with me. Logan Cooley becomes a superstar center. Dylan Guenther hits 50 goals. Dmitry Simashev asserts himself as a budding top-four defenseman. Anders Lee and Vincent Trocheck provide necessary veteran depth. Now open your eyes. Utah is your Western Conference champion.

Unfortunately for the Mammoth, they will run into a refreshed Panthers team that now has double-barreled Tkachuks. Last season, we found out that all it takes to stop Florida is to hope all of its key players miss significant time with injury. The long offseason was probably a blessing in disguise as it allowed the Panthers to get healthy, and that's bad news for everyone else. The structure in front of the crease will limit some of the volatility we saw from Jacob Markstrom in New Jersey, and Florida wins its third Stanley Cup in four years.

Baumgartner: Before last season, the Carolina Hurricanes' kryptonite was the Eastern Conference Final. The Hurricanes were able to overcome their demons and win the East before ultimately taking home the franchise's first Stanley Cup since 2006. In the West, the Dallas Stars have been running into the same problem. After three straight seasons ending in the Western Conference Final, the Stars regressed last postseason, flaming out in the first-round to the Minnesota Wild. The Central Division will once again be a gauntlet, but I'm ready for Dallas to take the next step and reach the Stanley Cup Final.

While Dallas has continued to fall short in recent years, the Panthers have done the opposite. The Panthers made three consecutive Stanley Cup Final appearances, winning back-to-back Cups in 2023-24 and 2024-25. Last season, Florida's three-peat attempt came to a halt on day one of training after captain Aleksander Barkov went down with a non-contact injury, tearing both the ACL and MCL in his right knee. An offseason of healing for Barkov, and the rest of the banged up Panthers, has the team refreshed entering September for the first time in years. With nearly the entire 2025 Stanley Cup winning core still on the team and Brady Tkachuk added to the fold, Florida looks ready to win its third Stanley Cup in four seasons.

Nivison: Celebrini should have won the Hart Trophy last season, but for some incomprehensible reason, he wasn't even a finalist. This year, Celebrini replicates that performance and gets San Jose to the postseason to take home his first MVP award at age 20. Hughes posts a career year in his first full season with the Wild, and Spencer Carberry gets his flowers for winning the Metro Division after missing the playoffs last year.

Baumgartner: McDavid's ability to take over a game was evident nearly every time he touched the ice last season. His 24 points (11 goals,13 assists) in the final 14 games of the regular season -- without Leon Draisaitl in the lineup -- was one of the many reasons I put him No. 1 on my Hart Trophy ballot. With just two years left on his current contract, desperation may start to kick in to get the job in Edmonton, which would lead to another MVP-caliber season. Barkov is healthy and the frontrunner to win his fourth Selke, and Dahlin will have another strong season to take home his first career Norris Trophy.

#2680. Brewers set World Series parade for Friday, two days after winning 2026 title

If not listed, please contact your TV provider.

In keeping with the ancient laws of combat, the Milwaukee Brewers are hereby entitled to a parade. The Brewers, with their win over the Tampa Bay Rays in Game 5 on Wednesday night, secured the World Series title for 2026 – their first such title in franchise history – and soon it'll be time to celebrate the occasion.

Speaking of which, the City of Milwaukee on Thursday announced plans for the Brewers' championship parade, which will take place on Friday, Oct. 30. Here are the essentials:

What: Brewers Parade of Champions and Championship RallyWhere: Starting at N. 4th St. and W. Wisconsin Ave. in downtown Milwaukee and ending at Henry Maier Festival ParkWhen: Starting at 10 a.m. CST on Friday, Oct. 30Cost: The parade is free and open to the publicTV/streaming: Nationally on MLB Network, MLB.tv, Brewers.tv, and Brewers.com (fuboTV, try for free); locally on CBS 58 starting at 9 a.m. CST.

The rally at Henry Maier Festival Park opens at 8 a.m. and will include large video screens featuring live parade coverage, live music, food and beverage offerings, exclusive merchandise and more. Brewers alumni will also be in attendance and the first 50,000 fans at the rally will receive a limited-edition championship rally towel.

🏆 Milwaukee, the celebration continues!Join the @Brewers for the Parade of Champions tomorrow, Friday, Oct. 30! 💙💛Starts at 10 AM at N. 4th St. & W. Wisconsin Ave., ending at Summerfest grounds for the Championship Rally!Full details: https://t.co/0r5qC7R61X pic.twitter.com/9CQJmK9Vzz

Milwaukee Public Schools also announced that school will be canceled for all students and staff on Friday for the parade and celebration.

Given the enthusiasm shown by Brewers fans throughout this season and especially the playoffs – they may have been the majority presence in Game 5 at Tropicana Field – it no doubt will be an enthusiastic and memorable celebration. Brewers supporters, after all, have been waiting 57 years for just such an event.
